\inf E_{1,>}^{(2)}-\sup E_{1,<}^{(2)}\leq 3\delta_{1}^{(2)},

then they belong to
Type

3
. Otherwise, each of them belongs to
Type

2
(c.f. Lemma
3.12
, Propositions
3.12
,
3.20
).

Hypothesis
5
holds true because the overlap of the adjacent codomains (
\sim 3\delta_{0}
) is much larger than the codomain contraction in our adjustment (c.f. Propositions
3.4
,
3.21
).

Finally, Hypothesis
6
by Theorem
3.22
.

y
J_{k}^{(2)}
I_{k,\cup}^{(2)}
E_{1}^{(2)}
E_{1}^{(1)}

Figure 8.
A cartoon output of the first induction step: A collection
\mathcal{C}_{1}
of local Rellich functions of
H_{B_{1}^{(j)}}
. The thick curves come from simple resonances, and the thin curves come from double resonances.
The adjacent domains of the curves have overlap
\gtrsim\delta_{0}
. The curves in blue (resp. green and red) belong to
Type

1
(resp.

2
and
3
). Note that different curves need not agree on the overlap of their domains.

3.5.
Construction of the next length scale

In this section, we suppose that Proposition
3.23
holds for
1\leq m\leq n
, and prove the Proposition for
m=n+1
. The inductive argument proceeds on each constructed function
E_{n}\in\mathcal{C}_{n}
. For this purpose, we fix a Rellich function
E_{n}\in\mathcal{C}_{n}
and construct the collection of its children
\mathcal{C}(E_{n})
. By our hypotheses,
E_{n}
has a two-monotonicity interval structure and satisfies the Morse condition. To classify the type of resonance, we need the following lemma:

Lemma 3.24
.

There exists at most one
0\neq\|{k_{n}}\|_{1}\leq 10l_{n+1}^{(1)}
, such that there is some
\theta_{{k_{n}},-}\in I(E_{n})_{-}
satisfying

\theta_{{k_{n}},-}+{k_{n}}\cdot\omega\in I(E_{n})_{+},\ e_{k_{n}}:=E_{n}(\theta_{{k_{n}},-})=E_{n}(\theta_{{k_{n}},-}+{k_{n}}\cdot\omega).

Moreover, if such a
{k_{n}}
exists, then
I(E_{n})_{+}
and
I(E_{n})_{-}
are two disjoint intervals and
E_{n}
belongs to
Type

1
.

Proof.

The proof is analogous to the proof of Lemma
3.3
. Since
|I(E_{n})_{\pm}|\lesssim\delta_{n-2}^{5}
(c.f. Hypothesis
1
) and
E_{n}
satisfies the Morse condition (c.f. Hypothesis
3
), we can employ Lemma
2.3
to show if there is another
{k^{\prime}_{n}}
and
\theta_{{k^{\prime}_{n}},-}
, then

(l_{n+1}^{(1)})^{-2\tau}\lesssim|e_{k_{n}}-e_{{k^{\prime}_{n}}}|\leq|J(E_{n})|\lesssim\delta_{n-2}^{10},

a contradiction.

If
I(E_{n})
is a single interval, then

(l_{n+1}^{(1)})^{-2\tau}\lesssim\|(\theta_{{k_{n}},-}+{k_{n}}\cdot\omega)-\theta_{{k_{n}},-}\|\leq|I(E_{n})|\lesssim\delta_{n-2}^{5}

since
\theta_{{k_{n}},-}
and
\theta_{{k_{n}},-}+{k_{n}}\cdot\omega\in I(E_{n})
, a contradiction.
∎

Define the double resonant interval (if exists)

J^{(2)}_{k_{n}}:=\bar{B}_{\delta_{n-1}^{10}}(e_{k_{n}})

and

J^{SR}_{n}:=\tilde{J}(E_{n})\setminus\bar{B}_{\delta_{n-1}^{10}-3\delta_{n}}(e_{k_{n}}).

We now divide it into simple resonant intervals of comparable size to the double-resonant
interval:

Proposition 3.25
.

\tilde{\tilde{J}}(E_{n})\cap J(E_{n})
can be covered by closed intervals
J_{i}^{(1)}
and
J^{(2)}_{k_{n}}
(if needed), such that

(1)
.

\delta_{n-1}^{10}\leq|J_{i}^{(1)}|\leq 2\delta_{n-1}^{10}
.

(2)
.

J_{i}^{(1)}\subset J^{SR}_{n}.

(3)
.

J_{i}^{(1)}\cap J_{i^{\prime}}^{(1)}\neq\emptyset\Rightarrow|J_{i}^{(1)}\cap J_{i^{\prime}}^{(1)}|=3\delta_{n}.

(4)
.

J_{i}^{(1)}\cap J^{(2)}_{k_{n}}\neq\emptyset\Rightarrow|J_{i}^{(1)}\cap J^{(2)}_{k_{n}}|=3\delta_{n}.

(5)
.

The total number of such intervals does not exceed
2\delta_{n-1}^{-10}
.

(6)
.

For any
E\in J_{i}^{(1)}
,
\bar{B}_{2\delta_{n}}(E)\subset\tilde{J}(E_{n})
.

(7)
.

\bar{B}_{5D\delta_{n-1}^{5}}(e_{k_{n}})\subset\tilde{J}(E_{n})
.

(8)
.

Moreover, if
E_{n}
belongs to
Type

3
, the closed intervals can be constructed such that

[\sup J(E_{n,<})-\frac{1}{2}\delta_{n-1}^{10},\inf J(E_{n,>})+\frac{1}{2}\delta_{n-1}^{10}]\subset J_{i_{n}}^{(1)}

(3.29)

for some
i_{n}
, and

[\sup J(E_{n,<})-\frac{1}{2}\delta_{n-1}^{10},\inf J(E_{n,>})+\frac{1}{2}\delta_{n-1}^{10}]\cap J_{i}^{(1)}=\emptyset

for any
i\neq i_{n}
.

Proof.

Since
|\tilde{J}(E_{n})|\sim\delta_{n-2}^{10}\gg\delta_{n-1}^{10}
, we can construct a collection of closed intervals of size between
\delta_{n-1}^{10}
and
2\delta_{n-1}^{10}
overlapping with only their nearest neighbors by exactly
3\delta_{n}
, together with the interval (if
\bar{B}_{5D\delta_{n-1}^{5}}(e_{k_{n}})\subset\tilde{J}(E_{n})
)

J^{(2)}_{k_{n}}
to cover
\tilde{J}(E_{n})\cap J(E_{n})
, with the possible exception of intervals of length at most
2\delta_{n-1}^{10}
at the boundaries of
\tilde{J}(E_{n})\cap J(E_{n})
where
E_{n}
does not attain a critical point. Since
2\delta_{n-1}^{10}<\frac{1}{8}\delta_{n-1}
, they thus cover
\tilde{\tilde{J}}(E_{n})\cap J(E_{n})
. If
E_{n}
belongs to
Type

3
, by the assumption, the gap between
\inf J(E_{m,>})
and
\sup J(E_{m,<})
does not exceed
3\delta_{n}
. We can define

J_{i_{n}}^{(1)}=[\sup J(E_{n,<})-\frac{1}{2}\delta_{n-1}^{10}-3\delta_{n},\inf J(E_{n,>})+\frac{1}{2}\delta_{n-1}^{10}+3\delta_{n}]

with
\delta_{n-1}^{10}\leq|J_{i_{n}}^{(1)}|\leq 2\delta_{n-1}^{10}
and construct the other intervals.
∎

The preimages of
J_{i}^{(j)}
are

I^{(1)}_{i,\pm}:=(E_{n})_{\pm}^{-1}(J^{(1)}_{i}),\ I^{(1)}_{i}:=I^{(1)}_{i,-}\cup I^{(1)}_{i,+}

and

I^{(2)}_{k_{n},\pm}:=(E_{n})_{\pm}^{-1}(J^{(2)}_{k_{n}}),\ I^{(2)}_{k_{n}}:=I^{(2)}_{k_{n},-}\cup I^{(2)}_{k_{n},+}.

Since
E_{n}
satisfies the Morse condition, by Lemma
2.3
, we have

2|I^{(j)}_{\bullet,\pm}|^{2}\leq|E_{n}(I^{(j)}_{\bullet,\pm})|=|J^{(j)}_{\bullet}|\leq D|I^{(j)}_{\bullet,\pm}|.

Thus

\delta_{n-1}^{10}\lesssim|I^{(j)}_{\bullet,\pm}|\leq 2\delta_{n-1}^{5},

(3.30)

where
\bullet\in\{i,k_{n}\}
.

3.5.1.
The simple resonance case

In this section, we fix an interval
J_{i}^{(1)}
and its preimage
I_{i}^{(1)}
to construct the Rellich child(ren) locally defined on
I_{i}^{(1)}
of
E_{n}
.

Proposition 3.26
.

If
\theta\in I^{(1)}_{i}
, then for any
0<\|x\|_{1}\leq 10l_{n+1}^{(1)}
such that
\theta+x\cdot\omega\in I(E_{n})
, we have

|E_{n}(\theta)-E_{n}(\theta+x\cdot\omega)|\geq 3\delta_{n}.

Proof.

Suppose
\theta\in I_{i,-}^{(1)}
(the case
\theta\in I_{i,+}^{(1)}
is completely analogous). If
\theta+x\cdot\omega\in I(E_{n})_{-}
, since
0<\|x\|_{1}\leq 10l_{n+1}^{(1)}
, by the Morse condition of
E_{n}
, we get

|E_{n}(\theta)-E_{n}(\theta+x\cdot\omega)|\geq\|x\cdot\omega\|^{2}\gtrsim(l_{n+1}^{(1)})^{-2\tau}\gg 3\delta_{n}.

We thus suppose
\theta+x\cdot\omega\in I(E_{n})_{+}
. Since
E_{n}
satisfies the two-monotonicity interval structure and has the same image on each monotonicity component, it must be
x=k_{n}
and there exists some
\theta_{k_{n},-}\in I(E_{n})_{-}
satisfying

\theta_{k_{n},-}+k_{n}\cdot\omega\in I(E_{n})_{+},\ e_{k_{n}}:=E_{n}(\theta_{k_{n},-})=E_{n}(\theta_{k_{n},-}+k_{n}\cdot\omega).

Assume
\theta\leq\theta_{k_{n},-}
. Then
\theta+k_{n}\cdot\omega\leq\theta_{k_{n},+}
. By the monotonicity of
E_{n}
on
I(E_{n})_{\pm}
,
E_{n}(\theta)\geq e_{k_{n}}
and
E_{n}(\theta+k_{n}\cdot\omega)\leq e_{k_{n}}
. Since
E_{n}(I_{i}^{(1)})=J_{i}^{(1)}\subset J^{SR}_{n}
,

E_{n}(\theta)-E_{n}(\theta+k_{n}\cdot\omega)\geq E_{n}(\theta)-e_{k_{n}}\geq\delta_{n-1}^{10}-3\delta_{n}>3\delta_{n}.

If
\theta\geq\theta_{k_{n},-}
, then similarly
E_{n}(\theta)\leq e_{k_{n}},E_{n}(\theta+k_{n}\cdot\omega)\geq e_{k_{n}}
, and

|E_{n}(\theta)-E_{n}(\theta+k_{n}\cdot\omega)|=E_{n}(\theta+k_{n}\cdot\omega)-E_{n}(\theta)\geq e_{k_{n}}-E_{n}(\theta)>3\delta_{n}.

∎

We choose a block
B_{n+1}^{(1)}
satisfying

\Lambda_{l_{n+1}^{(1)}}\subset B_{n+1}^{(1)}\subset\Lambda_{l_{n+1}^{(1)}+50l_{n}}

such that for all
\theta\in I_{i}^{(1)}
,
B_{n+1}^{(1)}
is
n
-strongly regular relative to
(\theta,E)
for all
E\in B_{2\delta_{n}}(E_{n}(\theta))
.

The simple resonance case will be divided into two subcases.

Subcase A.

E_{n}
does not belong to
Type

3
or
J_{i}^{(1)}
does not satisfy (
3.29
).

In this subcase, by
Hypothesis

4
,
E_{n}(\theta)
is the unique eigenvalue of
H_{B_{n}}(\theta)
in the interval
[E_{n}(\theta)-3\delta_{n},E_{n}(\theta)+3\delta_{n}]
for any
\theta\in I_{i}^{(1)}
. Thus we have

\|G_{n}^{\perp}(E)\|\lesssim\delta_{n}^{-1}

(3.31)

for any
|E-E_{n}(\theta)|\leq 2\delta_{n}
, where
G_{n}^{\perp}
is the Green’s function for
B_{n}
on the orthogonal complement of the eigenspace corresponding to
E_{n}(\theta)
. We will show that
E_{n}
has a unique Rellich child
E_{n+1}^{(1)}
of the Dirichlet restriction
H_{B_{n+1}^{(1)}}
, which is
C^{2}
-closed to
E_{n}
and belongs to
Type

1
or
2
.

Proposition 3.27
.

For
\theta\in I^{(1)}_{i}
, we have

(a).

H_{B_{n+1}^{(1)}}(\theta)
has a unique eigenvalue
E_{n+1}^{(1)}(\theta)
such that
|E_{n+1}^{(1)}(\theta)-E_{n}(\theta)|\lesssim e^{-\frac{\gamma_{0}}{4}l_{n}}\ll\delta_{n}^{10}
. Moreover, any other
\hat{E}\in\sigma(H_{B_{n+1}^{(1)}}(\theta))
must obey
|\hat{E}-E_{n}(\theta)|>2\delta_{n}
.

(b).

The corresponding eigenfunction
\psi_{n+1}
to
E_{n+1}^{(1)}
satisfies

|\psi_{n+1}(x)|\leq e^{-\frac{\gamma_{0}}{4}\|x\|_{1}},\ \|x\|_{1}\geq l_{n}^{\frac{6}{7}}.

(3.32)

(c).

Let
\psi_{n}
be the eigenfunction corresponding to
E_{n}(\theta)
of
H_{B_{n}}(\theta)
. Then up to a choice of sign,

\|\psi_{n+1}-\psi_{n}\|\lesssim e^{-\frac{\gamma_{0}}{4}l_{n}}\delta_{n}^{-1}.

(3.33)

(d).

\|G_{n+1}^{\perp}(E_{n+1}^{(1)})\|\leq\delta_{n}^{-1}
, where
G_{n+1}^{\perp}
denotes the Green’s function for
B_{n+1}^{(1)}
on the orthogonal complement of
\psi_{n+1}
.

Proof.

By the decay (
3.28
) of eigenfunction in the inductive hypothesis, we have

\|(H_{B_{n+1}^{(1)}}(\theta)-E_{n}(\theta))\psi_{n}\|=\|\Gamma_{n}\psi_{n}\|\lesssim e^{-\frac{\gamma_{0}}{4}l_{n}}.

The above estimate together with Lemma
2.2
shows the existence of
E_{n+1}^{(1)}(\theta)
.

Let
E\in B_{2\delta_{n}}(E_{n}(\theta))\subset\tilde{J}(E_{n})
and
\hat{B}_{n}
be a
O(l_{n}^{2/3})
-size block centered at origin so that
\Lambda:=B_{n+1}^{(1)}\setminus\hat{B}_{n}
is
n
-regular relative to
(\theta,E)
. By Proposition
3.26
,
we have
\Lambda\cap S_{n}(\theta,E)=\emptyset
. Thus
\Lambda
is
n
-good relative to
(\theta,E)
. By the
Hypothesis

6
, we obtain

\ |G_{\Lambda}(\theta,E;x,y)|\leq e^{-\gamma_{n}\|x-y\|_{1}},\ \|x-y\|_{1}\geq l_{n}^{\frac{5}{6}}.

Let
E\in\sigma(H_{B_{n+1}^{(1)}}(\theta))
such that
|E-E_{n}(\theta)|\leq 2\delta_{n}
. We determine the value of
\psi_{1}(x)
for
\|x\|\geq l_{n}^{\frac{6}{7}}
by

\psi_{n+1}(x)=\sum_{z,z^{\prime}}G_{\Lambda}(E,\theta;x,z)\Gamma_{z,z^{\prime}}\psi_{n+1}(z^{\prime}).

Since
\operatorname{dist}(x,\partial\hat{B}_{n})\geq\|x\|_{1}-O(l_{n}^{2/3})\geq\frac{2}{3}\|x\|_{1}>l_{n}^{5/6}
, by the off-diagonal exponential decay of
G_{\Lambda}
, we get

\displaystyle|\psi_{n+1}(x)|

\displaystyle\leq C(d)\sum_{z^{\prime}\in\partial^{+}\hat{B}_{n}}e^{-\frac{1}{3}\gamma_{0}\|x\|_{1}}|\psi_{n+1}(z^{\prime})|

\displaystyle\leq e^{-\frac{1}{4}\gamma_{0}\|x\|_{1}}.

Thus we finish the proof of
(b)
.

To establish
(c)
, we must show that
\psi_{n+1}
is close to
\psi_{n}
inside
B_{n}
. To see this, we restrict the equation
H_{B_{n+1}^{(1)}}(\theta)\psi_{n+1}=E_{n+1}^{(1)}(\theta)\psi_{n+1}
on
B_{n}
to get

\left(H_{B_{n}}-E_{n+1}^{(1)}\right)\psi_{n+1}=\Gamma_{B_{n}}\psi_{n+1}.

By (
3.31
), (
3.32
) and the above equation, we get

\|P_{n}^{\perp}\psi_{n+1}\|=\|G_{n}^{\perp}(E_{n+1}^{(1)})P_{n}^{\perp}\Gamma_{B_{n}}\psi_{n+1}\|\lesssim e^{-\frac{1}{4}\gamma_{0}l_{n}}\delta_{n}^{-1},

where
P_{n}^{\perp}
is the projection on the orthogonal complement of
\psi_{n}
. Since
\psi_{n+1}
is normalized, we obtain
\|\psi_{n+1}-\psi_{n}\|\lesssim e^{-\frac{1}{4}\gamma_{0}l_{n}}\delta_{n}^{-1}
up to a choice of sign.
If there is another
\hat{E}\in\sigma(H_{B_{n+1}^{(1)}}(\theta))
satisfying
|\hat{E}-E_{n}(\theta)|\leq 2\delta_{n}
, as above we can show that the corresponding eigenfunction
\hat{\psi}
must also satisfy (
3.33
) replacing
\psi_{n+1}
by
\hat{\psi}
, which violates the orthogonality. Thus we prove the uniqueness part of
(a)
.

Finally,
(d)
follows from any other
\hat{E}\in\sigma(H_{B_{n+1}^{(1)}}(\theta))
must obey

|\hat{E}-E_{n+1}^{(1)}(\theta)|\geq|\hat{E}-E_{n}(\theta)|-|E_{n}(\theta)-E_{n+1}^{(1)}(\theta)|\geq 2\delta_{n}-\delta_{n}\geq\delta_{n}.

∎

Proposition 3.28
.

We have the following:

|\frac{d^{s}}{d\theta^{s}}(E_{n+1}^{(1)}(\theta)-E_{n}(\theta))|\lesssim e^{-\frac{1}{16}\gamma_{0}l_{n}}\delta_{n}^{-s}\ll\delta_{n}^{10}\ {\rm for\ }s=0,1,2.

Proof.

By the previous proposition, we have

|E_{n+1}^{(1)}(\theta)-E_{n}(\theta)|\lesssim e^{-\frac{1}{4}\gamma_{0}l_{n}}.

(3.34)

Since (
3.33
), by Feynman-Hellman formula, we obtain

|(E_{n+1}^{(1)})^{\prime}(\theta)-E_{n}^{\prime}(\theta)|=|\left\langle\psi_{n+1},V^{\prime}\psi_{n+1}\right\rangle-\left\langle\psi_{n},V^{\prime}\psi_{n}\right\rangle|\lesssim e^{-\frac{\gamma_{0}}{4}l_{n}}\delta_{n}^{-1}.

For
s=2
, by Feynman-Hellman formula, we have

E_{n}^{\prime\prime}(\theta)=\left\langle\psi_{n},V^{\prime\prime}\psi_{n}\right\rangle-2\left\langle\psi_{n},V^{\prime}G_{n}^{\perp}(E_{n})V^{\prime}\psi_{n}\right\rangle,

(E_{n+1}^{(1)})^{\prime\prime}(\theta)=\left\langle\psi_{n+1},V^{\prime\prime}\psi_{n+1}\right\rangle-2\left\langle\psi_{n+1},V^{\prime}G_{n+1}^{\perp}(E_{n+1}^{(1)})V^{\prime}\psi_{n+1}\right\rangle.

Thus it suffices to estimate

\displaystyle|\left\langle\psi_{n+1},V^{\prime}G_{n+1}^{\perp}(E_{n+1}^{(1)})V^{\prime}\psi_{n+1}\right\rangle-\left\langle\psi_{n},V^{\prime}G_{n}^{\perp}(E_{n})V^{\prime}\psi_{n}\right\rangle|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle|\left\langle\psi_{n+1},V^{\prime}G_{n+1}^{\perp}(E_{n+1}^{(1)})V^{\prime}\psi_{n+1}\right\rangle-\left\langle\psi_{n},V^{\prime}G_{n+1}^{\perp}(E_{n+1}^{(1)})V^{\prime}\psi_{n}\right\rangle|

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle|\left\langle\psi_{n},V^{\prime}G_{n+1}^{\perp}(E_{n+1}^{(1)})V^{\prime}\psi_{n}\right\rangle-\left\langle\psi_{n},V^{\prime}G_{n}^{\perp}(E_{n})V^{\prime}\psi_{n}\right\rangle|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle O(e^{-\frac{\gamma_{0}}{4}l_{n}}\delta_{n}^{-2})+|\left\langle V^{\prime}\psi_{n},\left(G_{n+1}^{\perp}(E_{n+1}^{(1)})-G_{n}^{\perp}(E_{n})\right)V^{\prime}\psi_{n}\right\rangle|,

where we have used (
3.33
) and
\|G_{n+1}^{\perp}(E_{n+1}^{(1)})\|\leq\delta_{n}^{-1}
to bound the term on the second line.

It remains to estimate the operator
G_{n+1}^{\perp}(E_{n+1}^{(1)})-G_{n}^{\perp}(E_{n})
restricted to
B_{n}
. By resolvent identity, we have

\displaystyle G_{n+1}^{\perp}(E_{n+1}^{(1)})-G_{n}^{\perp}(E_{n})

(3.35)

\displaystyle=G_{n+1}^{\perp}(E_{n+1}^{(1)})P_{n}^{\perp}-P_{n+1}^{\perp}G_{n}^{\perp}(E_{n})+G_{n+1}^{\perp}(E_{n+1}^{(1)})P_{n}-P_{n+1}G_{n}^{\perp}(E_{n})

\displaystyle=G_{n+1}^{\perp}(E_{n+1}^{(1)})\left(-\Gamma_{n}+(E_{n+1}^{(1)}-E_{n})\right)G_{n}^{\perp}(E_{n})

\displaystyle+G_{n+1}^{\perp}(E_{n+1}^{(1)})P_{n+1}^{\perp}P_{n}-P_{n+1}P_{n}^{\perp}G_{n}^{\perp}(E_{n}).

We have used the orthogonal projection
P_{n}
and
P_{n+1}
onto
\psi_{n}
and
\psi_{n+1}
respectively and the relation
P_{n}+P_{n}^{\perp}=\operatorname{Id}_{B_{n}}
,
P_{n+1}+P_{n+1}^{\perp}=\operatorname{Id}_{B_{n+1}^{(1)}}
. The last two terms on the right hand side of (
3.35
) are bounded by
O(e^{-\frac{\gamma_{0}}{4}l_{n}}\delta_{n}^{-2})
since

\|P_{n+1}^{\perp}P_{n}\|=\|P_{n+1}^{\perp}\psi_{n}\|\leq 2\|\psi_{n+1}-\psi_{n}\|=O(e^{-\frac{\gamma_{0}}{4}l_{n}}\delta_{n}^{-1}),

\|P_{n+1}P_{n}^{\perp}\|=\|P_{n}^{\perp}P_{n+1}\|=\|P_{n}^{\perp}\psi_{n+1}\|\leq 2\|\psi_{n+1}-\psi_{n}\|=O(e^{-\frac{\gamma_{0}}{4}l_{n}}\delta_{n}^{-1})

and

\|G_{n}^{\perp}(E_{n})\|,\ \|G_{n+1}^{\perp}(E_{n+1}^{(1)})\|=O(\delta_{n}^{-1}).

The second term on the right hand side of (
3.35
) is bounded by
O(e^{-\frac{\gamma_{0}}{4}l_{n}}\delta_{n}^{-2})
since
|E_{n+1}^{(1)}-E_{n}|=O(e^{-\frac{\gamma_{0}}{4}l_{n}})
. Therefore the case
s=2
follows if we prove

\|\Gamma_{n}G_{n}^{\perp}(E_{n})V^{\prime}\psi_{n}\|=O(e^{-\frac{\gamma_{0}}{4}l_{n}}\delta_{n}^{-1}).

(3.36)

Let
\chi_{n}
be the restriction operator of the block
\Lambda_{\frac{l_{n}}{4}}\subset B_{n}
. By the estimate (
3.32
) we have

\|(1-\chi_{n})V^{\prime}\psi_{n}\|=O(e^{-\frac{\gamma_{0}}{16}l_{n}}).

Thus in order to prove (
3.36
), it suffices to show
\|\Gamma_{n}G_{n}^{\perp}\chi_{n}\|=O(e^{-\frac{\gamma_{0}}{16}l_{n}}\delta_{n}^{-1})
. To do this, we choose a
O(l_{n}^{2/3})
-size block
\hat{B}
centered at origin so that
A:=B_{n}\setminus\hat{B}
is
(n-1)
-good relative to
(\theta,E_{n}(\theta))
. By resolvent identity, we get

\displaystyle\Gamma_{n}G_{n}^{\perp}\chi_{n}

\displaystyle=\Gamma_{n}G_{A}\chi_{n}+\Gamma_{n}(\chi_{A}G_{n}^{\perp}-G_{A}P_{n}^{\perp})\chi_{n}-\Gamma_{n}G_{A}P_{n}\chi_{n}

\displaystyle=\Gamma_{n}G_{A}\chi_{n}+\Gamma_{n}G_{A}\Gamma_{A}G_{n}^{\perp}\chi_{n}-\Gamma_{n}G_{A}P_{n}\chi_{n}.

Since
A
is
(n-1)
-good, we have
\|G_{A}(E_{n})\|\leq 10\delta_{n-1}^{-1}
and
G_{A}(\theta,E_{n};x,y)
decays exponentially for
\|x-y\|_{1}\geq l_{n-1}^{\frac{5}{6}}
. Thus
\|\Gamma_{n}G_{A}\chi_{n}\|,\|\Gamma_{n}G_{A}\Gamma_{A}\|=O(e^{-\frac{\gamma_{0}}{16}l_{n}})
. To estimate the final term, we use
\|P_{n}\chi_{n}-\chi_{n}P_{n}\|\leq\|P_{n}\chi_{n}-P_{n}\|+\|P_{n}-\chi_{n}P_{n}\|\leq 2\|(1-\chi_{n})P_{n}\|=2\|(1-\chi_{n})\psi_{n}\|=O(e^{-\frac{\gamma_{0}}{16}l_{n}})
to obtain

\|\Gamma_{n}G_{A}P_{n}\chi_{n}\|\leq\|\Gamma_{n}G_{A}\chi_{n}P_{n}\|+\|\Gamma_{n}G_{A}\chi_{n}(P_{n}\chi_{n}-\chi_{n}P_{n})\|=O(e^{-\frac{\gamma_{0}}{16}l_{n}}\delta_{n}^{-1}).

∎

The next proposition verifies the Morse condition of
E_{n+1}^{(1)}(\theta)
.

Proposition 3.29
.

Assume
\theta\in I_{i}^{(1)}
such that
|(E_{n+1}^{(1)})^{\prime}(\theta)|\leq\delta_{n-1}^{2}
. Then

|(E_{n+1}^{(1)})^{\prime\prime}(\theta)|\geq 3-\sum_{s=0}^{n}\delta_{s}^{10}>2

with a unique sign for all such
\theta
.

Proof.

If
|(E_{n+1}^{(1)})^{\prime}(\theta)|\leq\delta_{n-1}^{2}
, by Proposition
3.28
, we have

|E_{n}^{\prime}(\theta)|\leq|(E_{n+1}^{(1)})^{\prime}(\theta)|+\delta_{0}^{10}\leq 2\delta_{n-1}<\delta_{n-2}^{2}.

By Hypothesis
3
,

|E_{n}^{\prime\prime}(\theta)|\geq 3-\sum_{s=0}^{n-1}\delta_{s}^{10}

with a unique sign for all such
\theta
. We employ Proposition
3.28
(the version
s=1
) to the above inequality to get

|(E_{n+1}^{(1)})^{\prime\prime}(\theta)|\geq|E_{n}^{\prime\prime}(\theta)|-\delta_{n}^{10}\geq 3-\sum_{s=0}^{n}\delta_{s}^{10}>2

with a unique sign for all such
\theta
.
∎

In the following proposition, we show each Rellich child
E_{n+1}^{(1)}
defined on
I^{(1)}_{i}
inherits the two-monotonicity interval structure and its derivative satisfies the estimates in Hypothesis
4
for
m=n+1
.

Proposition 3.30
.

We have the following:

(a).

Suppose
E_{n}
has a critical point in
I^{(1)}_{i}
. (Thus
I^{(1)}_{i}
is a closed interval.) Then
E_{n+1}^{(1)}
also has a critical point in
I^{(1)}_{i}
. Moreover,
I^{(1)}_{i}
can be divided into two closed intervals
\tilde{I}^{(1)}_{i,+}
and
\tilde{I}^{(1)}_{i,-}
with disjoint interiors, such that

\pm(E_{n+1}^{(1)})^{\prime}|_{\tilde{I}^{(1)}_{i,\pm}}\geq 0,\ \tilde{I}^{(1)}_{i,+}\cup\tilde{I}^{(1)}_{i,-}=I^{(1)}_{i}.

In this case,
E_{n+1}^{(1)}
belongs to
Type

2
.

(b).

Suppose
E_{n}
has no critical point in
I^{(1)}_{i}
(Thus
I^{(1)}_{i}=I^{(1)}_{i,+}\cup I^{(1)}_{i,-}
is a union of two closed disjoint intervals). Then

\pm(E_{n+1}^{(1)})^{\prime}|_{I^{(1)}_{i,\pm}}\gtrsim\delta_{n-1}^{10}.

In this case,
E_{n+1}^{(1)}
belongs to
Type

1
.

Proof.

To prove
(a)
, assume
\theta_{c}
is the critical point of
E_{n}
in
I^{(1)}_{i}
. By (
3.30
), we have
\operatorname{dist}(\theta_{c},\partial I^{(1)}_{i})\gtrsim\delta_{n-1}^{10}
. By Hypothesis
3
, we can employ Lemma
2.3
to obtain
|(E_{n})^{\prime}|_{\partial I^{(1)}_{i}}|\gtrsim\delta_{n-1}^{10}
with different signs. By Proposition
3.28
, we get

|(E_{n+1}^{(1)})^{\prime}|_{\partial I^{(1)}_{i}}|\geq|(E_{n})^{\prime}|_{\partial I^{(1)}_{i}}|-\delta_{n}^{10}\gtrsim\delta_{n-1}^{10}

with different sign on the edges of
I^{(1)}_{i}
. Since
(E_{n+1}^{(1)})^{\prime}
is continuous and
I^{(1)}_{i}
is connected, there is point
\theta_{s}\in I^{(1)}_{i}
such that
(E_{n+1}^{(1)})^{\prime}(\theta_{s})=0
. The two monotonicity structure follows from Proposition
3.29
and Lemma
2.3
immediately.

We prove
(b)
by case analysis:

(1).

E_{n}
belongs to
Type

1
: In this case, by Hypothesis
4
, we have

\pm(E_{n})^{\prime}|_{I^{(1)}_{i,\pm}}\gtrsim\delta_{n-2}^{10}.

Thus by Proposition
3.28
,

\pm(E_{n+1}^{(1)})^{\prime}|_{I^{(1)}_{i,\pm}}\geq\pm(E_{n})^{\prime}|_{I^{(1)}_{i,\pm}}-\delta_{n}^{10}\gtrsim\delta_{n-1}^{10}.

(2).

E_{n}
belongs to
Type

2
or
3
: In this case, we notice that from the construction (c.f. Proposition
3.25
), if
J_{i}^{(1)}
does not contain a critical value of
E_{n}
, then
J_{i}^{(1)}
has a distance on order
\delta_{n-1}^{10}
away from the critical value. Thus its preimage
I_{i}^{(1)}
has a distance on order
\delta_{n-1}^{10}
away from the critical point.
Since
E_{n}
satisfies the Morse condition, by Lemma
2.3
, we get

\pm(E_{n})^{\prime}|_{I^{(1)}_{i,\pm}}\gtrsim\delta_{n-1}^{10}.

The desired estimate for
\pm(E_{n+1}^{(1)})^{\prime}|_{I^{(1)}_{i,\pm}}
follows from the above estimate and Proposition
3.28
immediately.

∎

Subcase B.
The negation of
Subcase A
:
E_{n}
belongs to
Type

3
and
J_{i_{n}}^{(1)}
is the interval satisfying (
3.29
).

Before analyzing
Subcase B
, we are going to deal with the double resonance case, where we can extract some helpful information.

3.5.2.
The double resonance case

In this case, there exists some
k_{n}
with
0\neq\|k_{n}\|_{1}\leq 10l_{n+1}^{(1)}
and
\theta_{k_{n},-}\in I^{(2)}_{k_{n},-}
such that

\theta_{k_{n},-}+k_{n}\cdot\omega\in I^{(2)}_{k_{n},+},\ e_{k_{n}}:=E_{n}(\theta_{k_{n},-})=E_{n}(\theta_{k_{n},-}+k_{n}\cdot\omega),

(3.37)

and
E_{n}
belongs to
Type

1
by Lemma
3.24
.
Recall that

J^{(2)}_{k_{n}}:=\bar{B}_{\delta_{n-1}^{10}}(e_{k_{n}})

and

I^{(2)}_{k_{n},\pm}:=(E_{n})_{\pm}^{-1}(J^{(2)}_{k_{n}}),\ I^{(2)}_{k_{n}}:=I^{(2)}_{k_{n},-}\cup I^{(2)}_{k_{n},+}.

The domain of the Rellich children is defined as

I^{(2)}_{k_{n},\cup}:=I^{(2)}_{k_{n},-}\cup(I^{(2)}_{k_{n},+}-k_{n}\cdot\omega),

which is a single interval satisfying
|I^{(2)}_{k_{n},\cup}|\leq 4\delta_{n-1}^{5}
by (
3.30
). By the construction (c.f.
(7)
of Proposition
3.25
) and
|v^{\prime}|\leq D
, we have
I^{(2)}_{k_{n},\cup},I^{(2)}_{k_{n},\cup}+k_{n}\cdot\omega\subset I(E_{n})
. In the following, we denote by
E_{n-1}
the parent Rellich function of
E_{n}
.
We note that double resonance of
E_{n}
can happen only at length greater than
10l_{n}
:

Lemma 3.31
.

If such
k_{n}
exists, then
E_{n}
is generated from a simple resonant interval belonging to
Subcase A
of its parent
E_{n-1}
.

Proof.

Suppose that
E_{n}
is generated from a double resonant interval or the simple resonant interval belonging to
Subcase B
of its parent
E_{n-1}
. By the construction,
I(E_{n})
is a single interval of length of order at most
\delta_{n-2}^{5}\ll\|k_{n}\cdot\omega\|
by Diophantine condition, thus
\theta_{k_{n},-}
and
\theta_{k_{n},-}+k_{n}\cdot\omega
can not both belong to it, a contradiction.
∎

Lemma 3.32
.

The above
k_{n}
satisfies
10l_{n}<\|k_{n}\|\leq 10l_{n+1}^{(1)}
.

Proof.

Suppose that
\|k_{n}\|_{1}\leq 10l_{n}
. By the previous lemma and Proposition
3.28
,
E_{n}
is
C^{2}
-closed to
E_{n-1}
of order at most
\delta_{n-1}^{10}
. Since
E_{n}
belongs to
Type

1
, we have by Hypothesis
4
,

\pm E_{n}^{\prime}|_{I(E_{n})_{\pm}}\gtrsim\delta_{n-2}^{10}.

Thus

\pm E_{n-1}^{\prime}|_{I(E_{n})_{\pm}}\gtrsim\delta_{n-2}^{10}.

By the two-monotonicity interval structure and mean value theorem, there exists some point
\theta_{k_{n-1},-}
with

|\theta_{k_{n-1},-}-{\theta}_{k_{n},-}|\lesssim\delta_{n-1}^{10}\delta_{n-2}^{-10}<\delta_{n-1}^{9}

(3.38)

such that

E_{n-1}(\theta_{k_{n-1},-})=E_{n-1}(\theta_{k_{n-1},-}+k_{n-1}\cdot\omega).

Since
\|k_{n}\|_{1}\leq 10l_{n}
, by the construction,
\theta_{k_{n-1},-}
has a distance larger than
\delta_{n-1}^{9}
from any simple resonant domain of
E_{n-1}
. Thus by (
3.38
),
{\theta}_{k,-}
does not belong to any simple resonant domain of
E_{n-1}
, a contradiction with Lemma
3.31
.
∎

By the previous lemma, we have
B_{n}\cap(B_{n}+k_{n})=\emptyset
. Since
H_{B_{n}+k_{n}}(\theta)=H_{B_{n}}(\theta+k_{n}\cdot\omega)
,
H_{B_{n}+k_{n}}(\theta)
has an eigenvalue
E_{n}(\theta+k_{n}\cdot\omega)
. For convenience, we denote

B_{n,-}=B_{n},\ E_{n,-}(\theta)=E_{n}(\theta),

(3.39)

and the eigenfunction of
E_{n,-}
by
\psi_{n,-}
.
Similarly, we denote

B_{n,+}=B_{n}+k_{n},\ E_{n,+}(\theta)=E_{n}(\theta+k_{n}\cdot\omega),

(3.40)

and the eigenfunction of
E_{n,+}
by
\psi_{n,+}
.

Proposition 3.33
.

If
\theta\in I^{(2)}_{k_{n},\cup}
, then for any
\|x\|_{1}\leq(l_{n+1}^{(1)})^{4}
,
x\notin\{o,k_{n}\}
, we have

\theta+x\cdot\omega\notin I(E_{n-1}),\ n\geq 2,

|v(\theta)-v(\theta+x\cdot\omega)|\geq 2\delta_{0}^{\frac{1}{400}},\ n=1.

Proof.

For
n\geq 2
,
I(E_{n-1})
has at most two connected component with length at most
2\delta_{n-3}^{5}\ll(l_{n+1}^{(1)})^{-4\tau}
. Each of the component contains at least one point of
\{o,k_{n}\}
. The proof follows from Diophantine condition immediately.
The case
n=1
can be proved similarly as Proposition
3.11
.
∎

We choose a block
B_{n+1}^{(2)}
satisfying

\Lambda_{l_{n+1}^{(2)}}\subset B_{n+1}^{(2)}\subset\Lambda_{l_{n+1}^{(2)}+50l_{n}}

such that for all
\theta\in I_{k_{n},\cup}^{(2)}
,
B_{n+1}^{(2)}
is
(n-1)
-strongly regular relative to
(\theta,E)
for all
E\in B_{2\delta_{n-1}}(E_{n,-}(\theta))
.

Proposition 3.34
.

The following holds for
\theta\in I^{(2)}_{k_{n},\cup}
:

(a).

H_{B_{n+1}^{(2)}}(\theta)
has exactly two eigenvalues
E_{n+1}^{(2)}(\theta)
and
\mathcal{E}_{n+1}^{(2)}(\theta)
in
[E_{n,-}(\theta)-10D\delta_{n-1}^{5},E_{n,-}(\theta)+10D\delta_{n-1}^{5}]
. Moreover, any other
\hat{E}\in\sigma(H_{B_{n+1}^{(2)}}(\theta))
must obey
|\hat{E}-E_{n,-}(\theta)|\geq\delta_{n-1}
.

(b).

The corresponding eigenfunction of
E_{n+1}^{(2)}
(resp.
\mathcal{E}_{n+1}^{(2)}
)
,
\psi_{n+1}
(resp.
\Psi_{n+1}
)
decays exponentially fast away from
o
and
k_{n}
, i.e.,

|\psi_{n+1}(x)|\leq e^{-\frac{\gamma_{0}}{4}\|x\|_{1}}+e^{-\frac{\gamma_{0}}{4}\|x-k_{n}\|_{1}},

|\Psi_{n+1}(x)|\leq e^{-\frac{\gamma_{0}}{4}\|x\|_{1}}+e^{-\frac{\gamma_{0}}{4}\|x-k_{n}\|_{1}}

for
\operatorname{dist}(x,\{o,k_{n}\})\geq l_{n}^{6/7}.
Moreover, the two eigenfunctions can be expressed as

\displaystyle\begin{split}\psi_{n+1}=A\psi_{n,-}+B\psi_{n,+}+O(\delta_{n}^{10}),\\
\Psi_{n+1}=B\psi_{n,-}-A\psi_{n,+}+O(\delta_{n}^{10}),\end{split}

(3.41)

where
A,B
depend on
\theta
satisfying
A^{2}+B^{2}=1
.

(c).

\|G_{n+1}^{\perp\perp}(E_{n+1}^{(2)})\|,\|G_{n+1}^{\perp\perp}(\mathcal{E}_{n+1}^{(2)})\|\leq 2\delta_{n-1}^{-1}
, where
G_{n+1}^{\perp\perp}
denotes the Green’s function for
B_{n+1}^{(2)}
on the orthogonal complement of the space spanned by
\psi_{n+1}
and
\Psi_{n+1}
.

Proof.

We first consider the case
\theta^{*}=\theta_{k_{n},-}
. In this case, since
E_{n,-}(\theta^{*})=E_{n,+}(\theta^{*})
, by the decay of the eigenfunctions (c.f. (
3.28
)), we have

\|(H_{B_{n+1}^{(2)}}(\theta^{*})-E_{n,-}(\theta^{*}))\psi_{n,-}\|=\|\Gamma_{B_{n,-}}\psi_{n,-}\|\lesssim e^{-\frac{\gamma_{0}}{4}l_{n}}\ll\delta_{n}^{10},

\|(H_{B_{n+1}^{(2)}}(\theta^{*})-E_{n,+}(\theta^{*}))\psi_{n,+}\|=\|\Gamma_{B_{n,+}}\psi_{n,+}\|\lesssim e^{-\frac{\gamma_{0}}{4}l_{n}}\ll\delta_{n}^{10}.

Since
B_{n,-}
and
B_{n,+}
are disjoint,
\psi_{n,-}
and
\psi_{n,+}
serve as two trial wave functions for the operator
H_{B_{n+1}^{(2)}}(\theta^{*})-E_{n,-}(\theta^{*})
. It follows that by Lemma
2.2
,
H_{B_{n+1}^{(2)}}(\theta^{*})
has two eigenvalues in
[E_{n,-}(\theta^{*})-O(\delta_{n}^{10}),E_{n,-}(\theta^{*})+O(\delta_{n}^{10})]
. The claim for
\theta\in I^{(2)}_{k_{n},\cup}
follows from
|I^{(2)}_{k_{n},\cup}|\leq 4\delta_{n-1}^{5}
and
|v^{\prime}|\leq D
.

To establish the decay of eigenfunctions, assume
E\in\sigma(H_{B_{n+1}}^{(2)}(\theta))
with
|E-E_{n,-}(\theta)|\leq\delta_{n-1}
. We denote
\Lambda=B^{(2)}_{n+1}\setminus(B_{n-1}\cup(B_{n-1}+k_{n}))
, which is an
(n-1)
-regular set relative to
(\theta,E)
and
E_{n-1}
by the construction. Moreover, by Proposition
3.33
,
\Lambda
is
(n-1)
-nonresonant, thus
G_{\Lambda}(E)
decays exponentially, which together with restricting the eigenfunction equation to
\Lambda
yields the exponential decay of
\psi_{n+1}
.

To prove (
3.41
), it suffices to show
\psi_{n+1}
and
\Psi_{n+1}
are close to a linear combination of
\psi_{n,-}
and
\psi_{n,+}
inside
B_{n,-}\cup B_{n,+}
.
We restrict the equation
H_{B_{n+1}^{(2)}}(\theta)\psi_{n+1}=E_{n+1}^{(2)}(\theta)\psi_{n+1}
on
B_{n,-}
to get

\left(H_{B_{n,-}}-E_{n+1}^{(2)}\right)\psi_{n+1}=\Gamma_{B_{n,-}}\psi_{n+1}.

Thus

\|P_{n,-}^{\perp}\psi_{n+1}\|=\|G_{B_{n,-}}^{\perp}(E_{n+1}^{(2)})P_{n,-}^{\perp}\Gamma_{B_{n,-}}\psi_{n+1}\|=O(\delta_{n-1}^{-1}e^{-\frac{1}{4}\gamma_{0}l_{n}})\leq\delta_{n}^{10},

where
P_{n,-}^{\perp}=I-\langle\psi_{n,-}|\psi_{n,-}\rangle
is the projection onto the orthogonal complement of
\psi_{n,-}
and
G_{B_{n,-}}^{\perp}(E_{n+1}^{(2)})
is the Green’s function of
B_{n,-}
on
\operatorname{Image}P_{n,-}^{\perp}
with upper bound
O(\delta_{n-1}^{-1})
by
|E_{n,-}(\theta)-E_{n+1}^{(2)}(\theta)|\lesssim\delta_{n-1}^{5}
together with the eigenvalue separation result of simple resonance case (c.f. Proposition
3.27
replacing
n
by
n-1
).
Therefore inside
B_{n,-}
, we have

P_{n,-}^{\perp}\psi_{n+1}=O(\delta_{n}^{10}).

Thus

\psi_{n+1}\chi_{B_{n,-}}=a\psi_{n,-}+O(\delta_{n}^{10}).

where
a=\langle\psi_{n+1},\psi_{n,-}\rangle
.

Similarly,

\psi_{n+1}\chi_{B_{n,+}}=b\psi_{n,+}+O(\delta_{n}^{10})

with
b=\langle\psi_{n+1},\psi_{n,+}\rangle
.

Since the eigenfunction decays exponentially, we have

\|\psi_{n+1}\chi_{B^{(2)}_{n+1}\setminus(B_{n,-}\cup B_{n,+})}\|\leq\delta_{n}^{10}.

Thus we can write

\psi_{n+1}=a\psi_{n,-}+b\psi_{n,+}+O(\delta_{n}^{10}).

Taking norm of the above equation, we obtain
k:=a^{2}+b^{2}=1-O(\delta_{n}^{10})
. We set
A=a/k
and
B=b/k
. Hence
A^{2}+B^{2}=1
and
|A-a|,|B-b|=O(\delta_{n}^{10})
, which gives the desired expression of
\psi_{n+1}
. A similar argument gives
\Psi_{n+1}=C\psi_{n,-}+D\psi_{n,+}+O(\delta_{n}^{10})
with
C^{2}+D^{2}=1
. For convenience, we write
A=\cos\alpha,B=\sin\alpha,C=\sin\beta,D=-\cos\beta
. Since
\langle\psi_{n+1},\Psi_{n+1}\rangle=0
, we get
|\sin(\beta-\alpha)|=O(\delta_{n}^{10})
. We can choose
\beta
satisfying
|\beta-\alpha|=O(\delta_{n}^{10})
, thus
|B-C|=|\sin\alpha-\sin\beta|=O(\delta_{n}^{10})
and
|A+D|=|\cos\alpha-\cos\beta|=O(\delta_{n}^{10})
, which gives the desired expression
\Psi_{n+1}=B\psi_{n,-}-A\psi_{n,+}+O(\delta_{n}^{10})
. Assume
\hat{E}\in\sigma(H_{B_{n+1}}^{(2)}(\theta))
is a third eigenvalue in the interval
|\hat{E}-E_{n,-}(\theta)|\leq\delta_{n-1}
. The above argument still holds if we replace
E_{n+1}^{(2)}
by
\hat{E}
. Thus the eigenfunction
\hat{\psi}
corresponding to
\hat{E}
can also be expressed as

\hat{\psi}=\hat{A}\psi_{n,-}+\hat{B}\psi_{n,+}+O(\delta_{n}^{10})

with
\hat{A}^{2}+\hat{B}^{2}=1.
By orthogonality, we have
A\hat{A}+B\hat{B}=O(\delta_{n}^{10})
and
B\hat{A}-A\hat{B}=O(\delta_{n}^{10})
, a contradiction since
(A\hat{A}+B\hat{B})^{2}+(B\hat{A}-A\hat{B})^{2}=1
.
Hence any other third eigenvalue must obey
|\hat{E}-E_{n,-}(\theta)|\geq\delta_{n-1}
. Finally,
(c)
follows from
(a)
immediately.
∎

With the notation of the previous proposition, we denote

E_{n+1,>}^{(2)}(\theta)=\max(E_{n+1}^{(2)}(\theta),\mathcal{E}_{n+1}^{(2)}(\theta)),\ E_{n+1,<}^{(2)}(\theta)=\min(E_{n+1}^{(2)}(\theta),\mathcal{E}_{n+1}^{(2)}(\theta)).

Define

P_{n,\pm}:=\langle\psi_{n,\pm}|\psi_{n,\pm}\rangle,\ Q_{\pm}:=\operatorname{Id}_{B_{n+1}^{(2)}}-P_{n,\pm},

and consider the compressed operators

H_{\pm}(\theta):=Q_{\pm}H_{B_{n+1}^{(2)}}(\theta)Q_{\pm}.

Lemma 3.35
.

For
\theta\in I^{(2)}_{k_{n},\cup}
, the operators
H_{\pm}(\theta)
have unique eigenvalues
\lambda_{\mp}(\theta)
interlacing
E_{n+1,>}^{(2)}(\theta)
and
E_{n+1,<}^{(2)}(\theta)
:

E_{n+1,<}^{(2)}(\theta)\leq\lambda_{\mp}(\theta)\leq E_{n+1,>}^{(2)}(\theta).

(3.42)

Moreover,
\lambda_{\pm}(\theta)
are
C^{1}
-close to
E_{n,\pm}(\theta)
:

|\frac{d^{s}}{d\theta^{s}}(\lambda_{\pm}(\theta)-E_{n,\pm}(\theta))|\lesssim e^{-\frac{1}{5}\gamma_{0}l_{n}}\delta_{n-1}^{-s}\ll\delta_{n}^{10},\ s=0,1.

(3.43)

In particular, we have

\pm\lambda_{\pm}^{\prime}(\theta)\gtrsim\delta_{n-2}^{10}\gg\delta_{n-1}.

(3.44)

Proof.

We claim
H_{\pm}(\theta)
have unique eigenvalues
\lambda_{\mp}(\theta)
in
[E_{n,-}(\theta)-\delta_{n-1},E_{n,-}(\theta)+\delta_{n-1}]
satisfying

|\lambda_{\pm}(\theta)-E_{n,\pm}(\theta)|\lesssim e^{-\frac{1}{5}\gamma_{0}l_{n}}.

Since
Q_{\pm}\psi_{n,\mp}=\psi_{n,\mp}
,

\|(H_{\pm}(\theta)-E_{n,\mp}(\theta))\psi_{n,\mp}\|=\|Q_{\pm}\Gamma_{B_{n,\mp}}\psi_{n,\mp}\|\lesssim e^{-\frac{1}{5}\gamma_{0}l_{n}}.

The above estimate together with Lemma
2.2
. yields the existence of
\lambda_{\pm}
.

If
\hat{\lambda}
is an eigenvalue of
H_{\pm}(\theta)
satisfying
|\hat{\lambda}-E_{n,-}(\theta)|\leq\delta_{n-1}
, then its eigenfunction
\hat{\phi}\in\operatorname{Image}Q_{\pm}
satisfies

Q_{\pm}(H_{B_{n+1}^{(2)}}(\theta)-\hat{\lambda})\hat{\phi}=0.

Thus

(H_{B_{n+1}^{(2)}}(\theta)-\hat{\lambda})\hat{\phi}=a\psi_{n,\pm}.

Employing the same argument as the proof of item
(b)
of Proposition
3.34
together with the exponential decay of
\psi_{n,\pm}
, one can prove that

|\hat{\phi}(x)|\leq e^{-\frac{\gamma_{0}}{5}\|x\|_{1}}+e^{-\frac{\gamma_{0}}{5}\|x-k_{n}\|_{1}}

for
\operatorname{dist}(x,\{o,k_{n}\})\geq l_{n}^{7/8}
and
\hat{\phi}=\psi_{n,\mp}+O(e^{-\frac{1}{5}\gamma_{0}l_{n}}\delta_{n-1}^{-1})
since
\hat{\phi}\in\operatorname{Image}Q_{\pm}
.
Thus such an eigenvalue must be unique or else it will violate the orthogonality. By Theorem
3.14
and item
(a)
of Proposition
3.34
,
\lambda_{\mp}(\theta)
must lie between
E_{n+1,<}^{(2)}(\theta)
and
E_{n+1,>}^{(2)}(\theta)
.
Let
\phi_{\mp}\in\operatorname{Image}Q_{\pm}
be the eigenfunctions of
H_{\pm}
corresponding to
\lambda_{\mp}
. Since
\|\phi_{\mp}-\psi_{n,\mp}\|\lesssim e^{-\frac{1}{5}\gamma_{0}l_{n}}\delta_{n-1}^{-1}
, by the Feynman-Hellman formula
(1)
from Lemma
2.4
, we have

\displaystyle|\lambda_{\mp}^{\prime}-E_{n,\mp}^{\prime}|

\displaystyle=|\left\langle\phi_{\mp},(H_{\pm})^{\prime}\phi_{\mp}\right\rangle-\left\langle\psi_{n,\mp},H^{\prime}\psi_{n,\mp}\right\rangle|

\displaystyle=|\left\langle\phi_{\mp},V^{\prime}\phi_{\mp}\right\rangle-\left\langle\psi_{n,\mp},V^{\prime}\psi_{n,\mp}\right\rangle-2\langle\phi_{\mp},(P_{n,\pm})^{\prime}H_{B_{n+1}^{(2)}}\phi_{\mp}\rangle|

\displaystyle\lesssim e^{-\frac{1}{5}\gamma_{0}l_{n}}\delta_{n-1}^{-1}+|\langle\phi_{\mp},Q_{n,\pm}(P_{n,\pm})^{\prime}H_{B_{n+1}^{(2)}}Q_{n,\pm}\phi_{\mp}\rangle|.

Thus it suffices to estimate
Q_{n,\pm}P_{n,\pm}^{\prime}H_{B_{n+1}^{(2)}}Q_{n,\pm}
.
By differentiating the relation

(H_{B_{n,\pm}}-E_{n,\pm})\psi_{n,\pm}=0,

we get

(H_{B_{n,\pm}}-E_{n,\pm})\psi_{n,\pm}^{\prime}=-(V^{\prime}-E_{n,\pm}^{\prime})\psi_{n,\pm}.

Since
\langle\psi_{n,\pm}^{\prime},\psi_{n,\pm}\rangle=0
, we have

\psi_{n,\pm}^{\prime}=-G_{B_{n,\pm}}^{\perp}(E_{n,\pm})V^{\prime}\psi_{n,\pm}.

Thus

\displaystyle P_{n,\pm}^{\prime}

\displaystyle=\langle\psi_{n,\pm}|\psi_{n,\pm}^{\prime}\rangle+\langle\psi_{n,\pm}^{\prime}|\psi_{n,\pm}\rangle

\displaystyle=-G_{B_{n,\pm}}^{\perp}(E_{n,\pm})V^{\prime}P_{n,\pm}-P_{n,\pm}V^{\prime}G_{B_{n,\pm}}^{\perp}(E_{n,\pm}).

Since
P_{n,\pm}Q_{n,\pm}=Q_{n,\pm}P_{n,\pm}=0
, it follows that

Q_{n,\pm}P_{n,\pm}^{\prime}H_{B_{n+1}^{(2)}}Q_{n,\pm}=-Q_{n,\pm}G_{B_{n,\pm}}^{\perp}(E_{n,\pm})V^{\prime}P_{n,\pm}H_{B_{n+1}^{(2)}}Q_{n,\pm}.

(3.45)

We can employ the estimates

\displaystyle\|P_{n,\pm}H_{B_{n+1}^{(2)}}Q_{n,\pm}\|

\displaystyle=\|P_{n,\pm}(H_{B_{n+1}^{(2)}}-E_{n,\pm})Q_{n,\pm}\|

\displaystyle\leq\|P_{n,\pm}(H_{B_{n+1}^{(2)}}-E_{n,\pm})\|

\displaystyle\leq\|\Gamma_{B_{n,\pm}}\psi_{n,\pm}\|\lesssim e^{-\frac{1}{5}\gamma_{0}l_{n}}

and
\|G_{B_{n,\pm}}^{\perp}(E_{n,\pm})\|=O(\delta_{n-1}^{-1})
to (
3.45
) and complete the proof of (
3.43
).
Since
E_{n}
belongs to
Type

1
, by Hypothesis
4
, we have

\pm E_{n,\pm}^{\prime}\gtrsim\delta_{n-2}^{10}.

Finally, (
3.44
) follows from the above estimate and (
3.43
) immediately.
∎

Lemma 3.36
.

There is a unique point
\theta_{s}\in I^{(2)}_{k_{n},\cup}
satisfying
|\theta_{s}-\theta_{k_{n},-}|<\delta_{n}^{10}
, such that

\lambda_{+}(\theta_{s})=\lambda_{-}(\theta_{s}).

Moreover, the Rellich children have quantitative separation away from
\theta_{s}
:

E_{n+1,>}^{(2)}(\theta)-E_{n+1,<}^{(2)}(\theta)\geq|\lambda_{+}(\theta)-\lambda_{-}(\theta)|\geq\delta_{n-1}|\theta-\theta_{s}|.

(3.46)

As a corollary, if
E_{n+1}^{(2)}(\theta)=\mathcal{E}_{n+1}^{(2)}(\theta)
, then
\theta=\theta_{s}
.

Proof.

We consider the difference function

d(\theta):=\lambda_{+}(\theta)-\lambda_{-}(\theta).

Since
E_{n,+}(\theta_{k_{n},-})=E_{n,-}(\theta_{k_{n},-})
, by (
3.43
) (the version
s=0
), we have

|d(\theta_{k_{n},-})|\leq|\lambda_{+}(\theta_{k_{n},-})-E_{n,+}(\theta_{k_{n},-})|+|\lambda_{-}(\theta_{k_{n},-})-E_{n,-}(\theta_{k_{n},-})|\lesssim e^{-\frac{1}{5}\gamma_{0}l_{n}}.

By (
3.44
), we have

d^{\prime}(\theta)\geq\delta_{n-1}.

Thus there exists a unique point
\theta_{s}
satisfying
|\theta_{s}-\theta_{k_{n},-}|\lesssim e^{-\frac{1}{5}\gamma_{0}l_{n}}\delta_{n-1}^{-1}<\delta_{n}^{10}
, such that

d(\theta_{s})=0
, that is

\lambda_{+}(\theta_{s})=\lambda_{-}(\theta_{s}).

Then (
3.46
) follows from (
3.42
) and

|\lambda_{+}(\theta)-\lambda_{-}(\theta)|=|d(\theta)|=|d(\theta)-d(\theta_{s})|\geq\delta_{n-1}|\theta-\theta_{s}|

by mean value theorem.
∎

In the following, we assume that the Rellich functions are chosen so that
E_{n+1}^{(2)}>\mathcal{E}_{n+1}^{(2)}
on the right of
\theta_{s}
.

Suppose that
|E_{n,+}^{\prime}(\theta_{k_{n},-})\geq|E_{n,-}^{\prime}(\theta_{k_{n},-})|
(the argument is similar for the opposite case). Recalling the derivative estimate for
E_{n}
belonging to
Type

1
from Hypotheses
4
, we define
1\leq r\leq\delta_{n-1}^{-1}
such that

|E_{n,+}^{\prime}(\theta_{k_{n},-})|=r|E_{n,-}^{\prime}(\theta_{k_{n},-})|.

Lemma 3.37
.

For
\theta\in I_{k_{n},\cup}^{(2)}
, we have

|(E_{n,+}^{\prime}+rE_{n,-}^{\prime})(\theta)|\leq\delta_{n-1}^{2}

Proof.

Since
E_{n,+}^{\prime}
and
E_{n,-}^{\prime}
have opposite signs, we have

E_{n,+}^{\prime}(\theta_{k_{n},-})+rE_{n,-}^{\prime}(\theta_{k_{n},-})=0.

By the Feynman-Hellman formula
(2)
from Theorem
2.4
, we have the bound

|E_{n,\pm}^{\prime\prime}|\lesssim D+\|G_{B_{n,\pm}}(E_{n,\pm})\|\lesssim\delta_{n-1}^{-1},

where we employ the eigenvalue separation result of simple resonance case (c.f. Proposition
3.27
replacing
n
by
n-1
) to bound the term
\|G_{B_{n,\pm}}(E_{n,\pm})\|
in the above inequality.
Thus by mean value theorem, we have

\displaystyle|(E_{n,+}^{\prime}+rE_{n,-}^{\prime})(\theta)|

\displaystyle\leq(1+r)\delta_{n-1}^{-1}|\theta-\theta_{k_{n},-}|

\displaystyle\lesssim r\delta_{n-1}^{-1}|I_{k_{n},\cup}^{(2)}|

\displaystyle\leq\delta_{n-1}^{2}.

∎

Proposition 3.38
.

For
\theta\in I_{k_{n},\cup}^{(2)}
, we have the following:

(a).

If
E_{n+1}^{(2)}(\theta)\neq\mathcal{E}_{n+1}^{(2)}(\theta)
, then

\displaystyle(E_{n+1}^{(2)})^{\prime}

\displaystyle=(A^{2}-rB^{2})E_{n,-}^{\prime}+O(\delta_{n-1}^{2}),

(3.47)

\displaystyle(\mathcal{E}_{n+1}^{(2)})^{\prime}

\displaystyle=(B^{2}-rA^{2})E_{n,-}^{\prime}+O(\delta_{n-1}^{2}),

(3.48)

and

\displaystyle(E_{n+1}^{(2)})^{\prime\prime}

\displaystyle=\frac{2\left\langle\psi_{n+1},V^{\prime}\Psi_{n+1}\right\rangle^{2}}{E_{n+1}^{(2)}-\mathcal{E}_{n+1}^{(2)}}+O(\delta_{n-1}^{-1}),

(3.49)

\displaystyle(\mathcal{E}_{n+1}^{(2)})^{\prime\prime}

\displaystyle=\frac{2\left\langle\psi_{n+1},V^{\prime}\Psi_{n+1}\right\rangle^{2}}{\mathcal{E}_{n+1}^{(2)}-E_{n+1}^{(2)}}+O(\delta_{n-1}^{-1})

(3.50)

with the notation from Proposition
3.34
and
r
as above.

(b).

If
E_{n+1}^{(2)}(\theta)\neq\mathcal{E}_{n+1}^{(2)}(\theta)
and
|(E_{n+1}^{(2)})^{\prime}(\theta)|\leq\delta_{n-1}^{2}
, then
|(E_{n+1}^{(2)})^{\prime\prime}(\theta)|\geq 2
. Moreover, the sign of
(E_{n+1}^{(2)})^{\prime\prime}(\theta)
is the same as that of
E_{n+1}^{(2)}(\theta)-\mathcal{E}_{n+1}^{(2)}(\theta)
. The analogous conclusion holds if we exchange
E_{n+1}^{(2)}(\theta)
and
\mathcal{E}_{n+1}^{(2)}(\theta)
.

(c).

If there is a level crossing (thus
E_{n+1}^{(2)}(\theta_{s})=\mathcal{E}_{n+1}^{(2)}(\theta_{s})
by Lemma
3.36
), then for all
\theta\in I_{k_{n},\cup}^{(2)}
,

(E_{n+1}^{(2)})^{\prime}(\theta)>\delta_{n-1}^{2},\ (\mathcal{E}_{n+1}^{(2)})^{\prime}(\theta)<-\delta_{n-1}^{2}.

(3.51)

Proof.

We prove (
3.47
) (the proof of (
3.48
) is analogous). By (
3.41
) and Lemma
3.37
, we apply Feynman-Hellman formula
(1)
from Lemma
2.4
to obtain

\displaystyle(E_{n+1}^{(2)})^{\prime}

\displaystyle=\left\langle\psi_{n+1},V^{\prime}\psi_{n+1}\right\rangle=A^{2}\left\langle\psi_{n,-},V^{\prime}\psi_{n,-}\right\rangle+B^{2}\left\langle\psi_{n,+},V^{\prime}\psi_{n,+}\right\rangle+O(\delta_{n}^{10})

\displaystyle=A^{2}E_{n,-}^{\prime}+B^{2}E_{n,+}^{\prime}+O(\delta_{n}^{10})

\displaystyle=(A^{2}-rB^{2})E_{n,-}^{\prime}+B^{2}(E_{n,+}^{\prime}+rE_{n,-}^{\prime})+O(\delta_{n}^{10})

\displaystyle=(A^{2}-rB^{2})E_{n,-}^{\prime}+O(\delta_{n-1}^{2}).

To prove (
3.49
) and (
3.50
), we use Feynman-Hellman formulas
(2)
and
(3)
to obtain

(E_{n+1}^{(2)})^{\prime\prime}=\left\langle\psi_{n+1},V^{\prime\prime}\psi_{n+1}\right\rangle+2\frac{\left\langle\psi_{n+1},V^{\prime}\Psi_{n+1}\right\rangle^{2}}{E_{n+1}^{(2)}-\mathcal{E}_{n+1}^{(2)}}-2\left\langle V^{\prime}\psi_{n+1},G^{\perp\perp}_{n+1}(E_{n+1}^{(2)})V^{\prime}\psi_{n+1}\right\rangle.

The first term is bounded by
D
and the third term is bounded by

2\|G^{\perp\perp}_{n+1}(E_{n+1}^{(2)})\|\cdot\|V^{\prime}\psi_{n+1}\|^{2}\lesssim\delta_{n-1}^{-1},

where we use the estimate
\|G^{\perp\perp}_{n+1}(E_{n+1}^{(2)})\|\leq 2\delta_{n-1}^{-1}
from item
(c)
of Proposition
3.34
. Thus we finish the proof of item
(a)
.

Now we are going to prove
(b)
. We will show the first term in (
3.49
) is large if
|(E_{n+1}^{(2)})^{\prime}(\theta)|\leq\delta_{n-1}^{2}
.
Assuming
|(E_{n+1}^{(2)})^{\prime}(\theta)|\leq\delta_{n-1}^{2}
, by (
3.47
), we have

|A^{2}-rB^{2}||E_{n,-}^{\prime}(\theta)|\leq|(E_{n+1}^{(2)})^{\prime}(\theta)|+O(\delta_{n-1}^{2})=O(\delta_{n-1}^{2}).

Since

|E_{n,-}^{\prime}(\theta)|\gtrsim\delta_{n-2}^{10}>\delta_{n-1},

it follows that

|A^{2}-rB^{2}|\lesssim\delta_{n-1}^{2}\delta_{n-1}^{-1}<\frac{1}{100}.

Since
A^{2}+B^{2}=1
and
r\geq 1
, we obtain

(1+r)B^{2}\geq A^{2}+B^{2}-|A^{2}-rB^{2}|\geq\frac{99}{100}

and

(1+\frac{1}{r})A^{2}\geq A^{2}+B^{2}-\frac{1}{r}|A^{2}-rB^{2}|\geq\frac{99}{100}.

Thus

B^{2}\geq\frac{1}{4r},\ A^{2}\geq\frac{1}{4}.

By (
3.41
), Lemma
3.37
and the previous lower bound for
A,B
, we have

\displaystyle|\langle\psi_{n+1},V^{\prime}\Psi_{n+1}\rangle|

\displaystyle=|AB(\langle\psi_{n,-},V^{\prime}\psi_{n,-}\rangle-\langle\psi_{n,+},V^{\prime}\psi_{n,+}\rangle)+O(\delta_{n}^{10})|

\displaystyle=|AB(E_{n,-}^{\prime}-E_{n,+}^{\prime})+O(\delta_{n}^{10})|

\displaystyle=|AB\left((1+r)E_{n,-}^{\prime}-(E_{n,+}^{\prime}+rE_{n,-}^{\prime})\right)+O(\delta_{n}^{10})|

\displaystyle\geq\frac{1+r}{4\sqrt{r}}(|E_{n,-}^{\prime}|-O(\delta_{n-1}^{2}))

\displaystyle\gtrsim\delta_{n-2}^{10}\gg\delta_{n-1}.

By item
(a)
of Proposition
3.34
, we obtain the estimate of the denominator
|E_{n+1}^{(2)}-\mathcal{E}_{n+1}^{(2)}|\lesssim\delta_{n-1}^{5}
. Combining the previous estimate of numerator, by (
3.49
), we obtain

|(E_{n+1}^{(2)})^{\prime\prime}(\theta)|\gtrsim\delta_{n-1}^{2}\delta_{n-1}^{-5}-O(\delta_{n-1}^{-1})>2,

whose sign is the same as that of
E_{n+1}^{(2)}(\theta)-\mathcal{E}_{n+1}^{(2)}(\theta)
.

To prove
(c)
, we first show (
3.51
) holds for
\theta=\theta_{s}
. Since
E_{n+1}^{(2)}(\theta_{s})=\mathcal{E}_{n+1}^{(2)}(\theta_{s})
, by (
3.42
) we have

E_{n+1}^{(2)}(\theta_{s})=\mathcal{E}_{n+1}^{(2)}(\theta_{s})=\lambda_{+}(\theta_{s})=\lambda_{-}(\theta_{s}),

and since
E_{n+1}^{(2)}>\mathcal{E}_{n+1}^{(2)}
on the right of
\theta_{s}
, we have for
\theta>\theta_{s}
,

E_{n+1}^{(2)}(\theta)\geq\lambda_{+}(\theta)>\lambda_{-}(\theta)\geq\mathcal{E}_{n+1}^{(2)}(\theta).

Thus by (
3.44
),
we get

(E_{n+1}^{(2)})^{\prime}(\theta_{s})\geq\lambda_{+}^{\prime}(\theta_{s})>\delta_{n-1},\ (\mathcal{E}_{n+1}^{(2)})^{\prime}(\theta_{s})\leq\lambda_{-}^{\prime}(\theta_{s})<-\delta_{n-1}.

We next claim the inequalities hold for all
\theta\in I_{k_{n},\cup}^{(2)}
. We prove the case that
(E_{n+1}^{(2)})^{\prime}(\theta)
and
\theta>\theta_{s}
(the other cases are analogous). If it is not true, then the set

\{\theta\in I_{k_{n},\cup}^{(2)}:\ \theta>\theta_{s},\ (E_{n+1}^{(2)})^{\prime}(\theta)\leq\delta_{n-1}^{2}\}\neq\emptyset.

Let
\theta^{*}
be its infimum. Since
(E_{n+1}^{(2)})^{\prime}(\theta)
is continuous and
(E_{n+1}^{(2)})^{\prime}(\theta_{s})>\delta_{n-1}^{2}
, we have
\theta^{*}>\theta_{s}
and
(E_{n+1}^{(2)})^{\prime}(\theta)>\delta_{n-1}^{2}\geq(E_{n+1}^{(2)})^{\prime}(\theta^{*})
for
\theta\in[\theta_{s},\theta^{*})
, which implies
(E_{n+1}^{(2)})^{\prime\prime}(\theta^{*})\leq 0
. However, by item
(b)
and
E_{n+1}^{(2)}(\theta^{*})>\mathcal{E}_{n+1}^{(2)}(\theta^{*})
, we get
(E_{n+1}^{(2)})^{\prime\prime}(\theta^{*})>2
, a contradiction. Thus we prove our claim.
∎

Define

E_{n,\vee}(\theta)=\max(E_{n,+}(\theta),E_{n,-}(\theta)),\ E_{n,\wedge}(\theta)=\min(E_{n,+}(\theta),E_{n,-}(\theta)).

Proposition 3.39
.

For
\theta\in I_{k_{n},\cup}^{(2)}
, we have

|E_{n+1,>}^{(2)}(\theta)-E_{n,\vee}(\theta)|,|E_{n+1,<}^{(2)}(\theta)-E_{n,\wedge}(\theta)|\lesssim e^{-\frac{1}{4}\gamma_{0}l_{n}}.

(3.52)

Proof.

Fix
\theta\in I_{k_{n},\cup}^{(2)}
. We have

\|(H_{B_{n+1}^{(2)}}-E_{n,\pm})\psi_{n,\pm}\|=\|\Gamma_{B_{n,\pm}}\psi_{n,\pm}\|\lesssim e^{-\frac{1}{4}\gamma_{0}l_{n}}.

By Lemma
2.2
, we deduce
H_{B_{n+1}^{(2)}}
must have an eigenvalue in
[E_{n,\vee}-O(e^{-\frac{1}{4}\gamma_{0}l_{n}}),E_{n,\vee}+O(e^{-\frac{1}{4}\gamma_{0}l_{n}})]
and in
[E_{n,\wedge}-O(e^{-\frac{1}{4}\gamma_{0}l_{n}}),E_{n,\wedge}+O(e^{-\frac{1}{4}\gamma_{0}l_{n}})]
. By item
(a)
of Proposition
3.34
, the eigenvalue must be
E_{n+1}^{(2)}
or
\mathcal{E}_{n+1}^{(2)}
. If the two intervals are disjoint, (
3.52
) must hold.
Otherwise, we have

E_{n,\vee}-E_{n,\wedge}\lesssim e^{-\frac{1}{4}\gamma_{0}l_{n}}.

(3.53)

Thus we have two trial functions for
E_{n,\vee}
:

\|(H_{B_{n+1}^{(2)}}-E_{n,\vee})\psi_{n,\pm}\|\lesssim e^{-\frac{1}{4}\gamma_{0}l_{n}}.

By Lemma
2.2
,
H_{B_{n+1}^{(2)}}
must have two eigenvalues in
[E_{n,\vee}-O(e^{-\frac{1}{4}\gamma_{0}l_{n}}),E_{n,\vee}+O(e^{-\frac{1}{4}\gamma_{0}l_{n}})].
They are
E_{n+1}^{(2)}
and
\mathcal{E}_{n+1}^{(2)}
. Combining (
3.53
), we also obtain (
3.52
).
∎

The next proposition shows the two-monotonicity interval structure of
E_{n+1,>}^{(2)}
and
E_{n+1,<}^{(2)}
.

Proposition 3.40
.

We have the following:

(a).

If there is no level crossing, then
E_{n+1}^{(2)}=E_{n+1,>}^{(2)}
and
\mathcal{E}_{n+1}^{(2)}=E_{n+1,<}^{(2)}
. Moreover,
E_{n+1}^{(2)}
(resp.
\mathcal{E}_{n+1}^{(2)}
) has the two-monotonicity interval structure with a critical point
\theta_{>}
(resp.
\theta_{<}
) satisfying
|\theta_{>}-\theta_{k_{n},-}|<\delta_{n}^{10}
(resp.
|\theta_{<}-\theta_{k_{n},-}|<\delta_{n}^{10}
).

(b).

If there is a level crossing (thus
E_{n+1}^{(2)}(\theta_{s})=\mathcal{E}_{n+1}^{(2)}(\theta_{s})
), then

\displaystyle E_{n+1}^{(2)}(\theta)=\left\{\begin{aligned} &E_{n+1,<}^{(2)}(\theta)&\text{if $\theta<\theta_{s}$,}\\
&E_{n+1,>}^{(2)}(\theta)&\text{if $\theta>\theta_{s}$,}\end{aligned}\right.

and

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}_{n+1}^{(2)}(\theta)=\left\{\begin{aligned} &E_{n+1,>}^{(2)}(\theta)&\text{if $\theta<\theta_{s}$,}\\
&E_{n+1,<}^{(2)}(\theta)&\text{if $\theta>\theta_{s}$.}\end{aligned}\right.

Moreover,
E_{n+1,>}^{(2)}
and
E_{n+1,<}^{(2)}
are piecewise
C^{1}
functions (except at the point
\theta_{s}
) with two-monotonicity interval structure.

Combining the previous propositions of double resonance case, we deduce that if the gap between the critical values of
E_{n+1,>}^{(2)}
and
E_{n+1,<}^{(2)}
is larger than
3\delta_{n+1}
, then each of them belongs to
Type

2
, otherwise,
E_{n+1,>}^{(2)}
along with
E_{n+1,<}^{(2)}
belongs to
Type

3
.

Proof.

If there is no level crossing, then
E_{n+1,>}^{(2)}(\theta)>E_{n+1,<}^{(2)}(\theta)
for all
\theta\in I_{k_{n},\cup}^{(2)}
. Since
E_{n+1}^{(2)}>\mathcal{E}_{n+1}^{(2)}
on the right of
\theta_{s}
and by continuity,
E_{n+1}^{(2)}(\theta)>\mathcal{E}_{n+1}^{(2)}(\theta)
for all
\theta\in I_{k_{n},\cup}^{(2)}
. Thus
E_{n+1}^{(2)}=E_{n+1,>}^{(2)}
and
\mathcal{E}_{n+1}^{(2)}=E_{n+1,<}^{(2)}
.

Now we show the existence of the critical point
\theta_{>}
for
E_{n+1}^{(2)}
(the proof of
\theta_{<}
and
\mathcal{E}_{n+1}^{(2)}
is analogous). Let
\theta_{-}:=\theta_{k_{n},-}-\delta_{n}^{10}
and
\theta_{+}:=\theta_{k_{n},-}+\delta_{n}^{10}
. Recalling
\pm E_{n,\pm}^{\prime}\gtrsim\delta_{n-2}^{10}>\delta_{n}
and (
3.52
), we have by mean value theorem

E_{n+1}^{(2)}(\theta_{+})\geq E_{n,+}(\theta_{+})-O(e^{-\frac{1}{4}\gamma_{0}l_{n}})\geq E_{n,+}(\theta_{k_{n},-})+\delta_{n}^{11}-O(e^{-\frac{1}{4}\gamma_{0}l_{n}})>E_{n+1}^{(2)}(\theta_{k_{n},-})

and

E_{n+1}^{(2)}(\theta_{-})\geq E_{n,-}(\theta_{-})-O(e^{-\frac{1}{4}\gamma_{0}l_{n}})\geq E_{n,-}(\theta_{k_{n},-})+\delta_{n}^{11}-O(e^{-\frac{1}{4}\gamma_{0}l_{n}})>E_{n+1}^{(2)}(\theta_{k_{n},-}).

Thus
E_{n+1}^{(2)}
must have a critical point
\theta_{>}\in[\theta_{-},\theta_{+}]
by mean value theorem.
The two-monotonicity interval structure follows from item
(b)
of Proposition
3.38
and Lemma
2.3
. Thus we finish the proof of
(a)
.

If
E_{n+1}^{(2)}(\theta_{s})=\mathcal{E}_{n+1}^{(2)}(\theta_{s})
, by item
(c)
of Proposition
3.38
,
E_{n+1}^{(2)}
is strictly increasing and
\mathcal{E}_{n+1}^{(2)}
is strictly decreasing. Thus
E_{n+1}^{(2)}>\mathcal{E}_{n+1}^{(2)}
on the right of
\theta_{s}
and
E_{n+1}<\mathcal{E}_{n+1}^{(2)}
on the left of
\theta_{s}
. The proof of
(b)
is obvious.
∎

3.5.3.
Completion of the simple resonance case

In this part, we will complete the proof of
Subcase B
in the simple resonance case. In this subcase,
E_{n}
belongs to
Type

3
and the simple resonant interval we consider is

J_{i_{n}}^{(1)}=[\sup J(E_{n,<})-\frac{1}{2}\delta_{n-1}^{10}-3\delta_{n},\inf J(E_{n,>})+\frac{1}{2}\delta_{n-1}^{10}+3\delta_{n}].

(3.54)

Recall that if
E_{n}
belongs to
Type

3
, there exist two brother Rellich functions with
0\leq\inf E_{n,>}(\theta)-\sup E_{n,<}(\theta)\leq 3\delta_{n}
.

gap
\leq 3\delta_{n}
E_{n,>}
E_{n,<}
J_{i_{n}}^{(1)}
\underbrace{\hskip 71.13188pt}
\delta_{n-1}^{10}\sim

Figure 9.
A cartoon illustration of the interval
J_{i_{n}}^{(1)}
in
Subcase B
:
E_{n}
belongs to
Type 3
, and the gap between the Rellich brothers is smaller than
3\delta_{n}
.
J_{i_{n}}^{(1)}
with length
\sim\delta_{n-1}^{10}
covers the nearby region of the critical values of the two Rellich brothers.

Lemma 3.41
.

E_{n}
must have some ancestor
E_{s}\ (1\leq s\leq n)
generated from the double resonant interval of its parent
E_{s-1}
.

Proof.

For the sake of contradiction, assume that
E_{s}\in\mathcal{C}^{(1)}(E_{s-1})
for all
1\leq s\leq n
. By the previous analysis of the simple resonance case (c.f. Propositions
3.6
,
3.10
,
3.30
),
E_{n}
must belong to
Type

1
or
Type

2
, a contradiction.
∎

Let
m
be the largest integer of
0\leq m\leq n-1
such that
E_{m+1}
is generated from the double resonant interval of its parent
E_{m}
among the ancestors of
E_{n}
, that is, there exists some
k_{m}\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}
with
10l_{m}<\|k_{m}\|_{1}\leq 10l_{m+1}
satisfying

e_{k_{m}}:=E_{m}(\theta_{k_{m},-})=E_{m}(\theta_{k_{m},-}+k_{m}\cdot\omega)

for some
\theta_{k_{m},-}\in I(E_{m})_{-}
and
E_{m+1,>},E_{m+1,<}\in\mathcal{C}^{(2)}(E_{m})
are the Rellich children generated from the double resonant interval
\bar{B}_{\delta_{m-1}^{10}}(e_{k_{m}})
. Thus the results of the double resonance case from the previous section hold true with
m
replacing
n
.

Lemma 3.42
.

For all
m+1\leq s\leq n-1
,
E_{s}
belongs to
Type

3
and
E_{s+1}
is generated from the interval
J_{i_{s}}^{(1)}
((
3.54
) replacing
n
by
s
) of
E_{s}
.

Proof.

By the definition of
m
,
E_{s+1}\in\mathcal{C}^{(1)}(E_{s})
for all
m+1\leq s\leq n-1
. If the lemma is not true, as the proof of Lemma
3.41
,
E_{n}
must belong to
Type

1
or
Type

2
, a contradiction.
∎

Define the domains

I^{(1)}_{>}:=(E_{n,>})^{-1}\left(J_{i_{n}}^{(1)}\cap J(E_{n,>})\right),\ I^{(1)}_{<}:=(E_{n,<})^{-1}\left(J_{i_{n}}^{(1)}\cap J(E_{n,<})\right)

and

I^{(1)}_{\cap}:=I^{(1)}_{>}\cap I^{(1)}_{<},\ I^{(1)}_{\cup}:=I^{(1)}_{>}\cup I^{(1)}_{<}.

Lemma 3.43
.

I^{(1)}_{\cap}\neq\emptyset
. Thus
I^{(1)}_{\cup}
is a single interval with
\delta_{n-1}^{10}\lesssim|I^{(1)}_{\cup}|\lesssim\delta_{n-1}^{5}
. Moreover, let
\theta_{n,>}
(resp.
\theta_{n,<}
) be the critical point of
E_{n,>}
(resp.
E_{n,<}
), then
\theta_{n,>},\theta_{n,<}\in I^{(1)}_{\cap}
.

Proof.

We assume that
\theta_{n,>}\geq\theta_{n,<}
. (The other case is analogous.) Recalling Hypothesis
4
, there exist two interlacing functions
\lambda_{n,\pm}
satisfying

E_{n,<}(\theta)\leq\lambda_{n,\pm}(\theta)\leq E_{n,>}(\theta),

\pm\lambda_{n,\pm}^{\prime}(\theta)\geq\delta_{n-2}.

Thus

\displaystyle\delta_{n-2}(\theta_{n,>}-\theta_{n,<})

\displaystyle\leq\lambda_{n,+}(\theta_{n,>})-\lambda_{n,+}(\theta_{n,<})

\displaystyle\leq E_{n,>}(\theta_{n,>})-E_{n,<}(\theta_{n,<})\leq 3\delta_{n}.

From the above inequality, we obtain

|\theta_{n,>}-\theta_{n,<}|\leq 3\delta_{n}\delta_{n-2}^{-1}\ll\delta_{n-1}^{10}.

(3.55)

Since
|J_{i_{n}}^{(1)}\cap J(E_{n,>})|\geq\frac{1}{2}\delta_{n-1}^{10}
, by the uniform bound of
|v^{\prime}|
, we have

B_{\frac{\delta_{n-1}^{10}}{2D}}(\theta_{n,>})\subset I^{(1)}_{>}.

(3.56)

Similarly,

B_{\frac{\delta_{n-1}^{10}}{2D}}(\theta_{n,<})\subset I^{(1)}_{<}.

(3.57)

The lemma follows from (
3.55
), (
3.56
) and (
3.57
) immediately.
∎

We choose a block a block
B_{n+1}^{(1)}
satisfying

\Lambda_{l_{n+1}^{(1)}}\subset B_{n+1}^{(1)}\subset\Lambda_{l_{n+1}^{(1)}+50l_{n}}

such that for all
\theta\in I^{(1)}_{\cup}
,
B_{n+1}^{(1)}
is
(n-1)
-strongly regular relative to
(\theta,E)
for all
E\in B_{2\delta_{n-1}}(E_{n,>}(\theta))
.

Proposition 3.44
.

The following holds for
\theta\in I^{(1)}_{\cup}
:

(a).

H_{B_{n+1}^{(1)}}(\theta)
has two eigenvalues
E_{n+1,>}(\theta)
and
E_{n+1,<}(\theta)
with
E_{n+1,>}(\theta)\geq E_{n+1,<}(\theta)
satisfying

\displaystyle\begin{split}|E_{n+1,>}(\theta)-E_{n,>}(\theta)|\lesssim e^{-\frac{\gamma_{0}}{4}l_{n}}\ll\delta_{n}^{10},\\
|E_{n+1,<}(\theta)-E_{n,<}(\theta)|\lesssim e^{-\frac{\gamma_{0}}{4}l_{n}}\ll\delta_{n}^{10}.\end{split}

(3.58)

Moreover, any other
\hat{E}\in\sigma(H_{B_{n+1}^{(1)}}(\theta))
must obey
|\hat{E}-E_{n,>}(\theta)|\geq\delta_{n-1}
.

(b).

The corresponding eigenfunction of
E_{n+1,>}
(resp.
E_{n+1,<}
)
,
\psi_{n+1,>}
(resp.
\psi_{n+1,<}
)
decays exponentially fast away from
o
and
k_{m}
, i.e.,

|\psi_{n+1,>}(x)|\leq e^{-\frac{\gamma_{0}}{4}\|x\|_{1}}+e^{-\frac{\gamma_{0}}{4}\|x-k_{m}\|_{1}},

|\psi_{n+1,<}(x)|\leq e^{-\frac{\gamma_{0}}{4}\|x\|_{1}}+e^{-\frac{\gamma_{0}}{4}\|x-k_{m}\|_{1}}

for
\operatorname{dist}(x,\{o,k_{m}\})\geq l_{m}^{6/7}.
Moreover, the two eigenfunctions can be expressed as

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\psi_{n+1,>}=A\psi_{m,-}+B\psi_{m,+}+O(\delta_{m}^{10}),\\
&\psi_{n+1,<}=B\psi_{m,-}-A\psi_{m,+}+O(\delta_{m}^{10}),\end{split}

(3.59)

where
A,B
depend on
\theta
satisfying
A^{2}+B^{2}=1
.

(c).

\|G_{n+1}^{\perp\perp}(E_{n+1,>})\|,\|G_{n+1}^{\perp\perp}(E_{n+1,<})\|\leq 2\delta_{n-1}^{-1}
, where
G_{n+1}^{\perp\perp}
denotes the Green’s function for
B_{n+1}^{(1)}
on the orthogonal complement of the space spanned by
\psi_{n+1,>}
and
\psi_{n+1,<}
.

Proof.

Fix
\theta\in I_{\cup}^{(1)}
. Denote by
\psi_{n,>}
(resp.
\psi_{n,<}
) the eigenfunction corresponding to
E_{n,>}
(resp.
E_{n,<}
) of the restriction operator
H_{B_{n}}
. By the exponential decay (
3.28
) of the eigenfunctions from the inductive hypothesis, we have

\|(H_{B_{n+1}^{(1)}}-E_{n,>})\psi_{n,>}\|=\|\Gamma_{B_{n}}\psi_{n,>}\|\lesssim e^{-\frac{1}{4}\gamma_{0}l_{n}},

\|(H_{B_{n+1}^{(1)}}-E_{n,<})\psi_{n,<}\|=\|\Gamma_{B_{n}}\psi_{n,<}\|\lesssim e^{-\frac{1}{4}\gamma_{0}l_{n}}.

The above estimates together with Lemma
2.2
yield
H_{B_{n+1}^{(1)}}
must have an eigenvalue in
[E_{n,>}-O(e^{-\frac{1}{4}\gamma_{0}l_{n}}),E_{n,>}+O(e^{-\frac{1}{4}\gamma_{0}l_{n}})]
and in
[E_{n,<}-O(e^{-\frac{1}{4}\gamma_{0}l_{n}}),E_{n,<}+O(e^{-\frac{1}{4}\gamma_{0}l_{n}})]
. If the two intervals are disjoint, (
3.58
) must hold.
Otherwise, we have

E_{n,>}-E_{n,<}\lesssim e^{-\frac{1}{4}\gamma_{0}l_{n}}.

(3.60)

Thus we have two trial functions for
E_{n,>}
:

\|(H_{B_{n+1}^{(1)}}-E_{n,>})\psi_{n,>}\|\lesssim e^{-\frac{1}{4}\gamma_{0}l_{n}},

\|(H_{B_{n+1}^{(1)}}-E_{n,>})\psi_{n,<}\|\leq|E_{n,>}-E_{n,<}|+\|(H_{B_{n+1}^{(1)}}-E_{n,<})\psi_{n,<}\|\lesssim e^{-\frac{1}{4}\gamma_{0}l_{n}}.

By Lemma
2.2
,
H_{B_{n+1}^{(1)}}
must have two eigenvalues in
[E_{n,>}-O(e^{-\frac{1}{4}\gamma_{0}l_{n}}),E_{n,>}+O(e^{-\frac{1}{4}\gamma_{0}l_{n}})].
Combining (
3.60
), we also obtain (
3.58
). Thus we finish the existence part of
(a)
.

We now prove
(b)
. Assume
E\in\sigma(H_{B_{n+1}}^{(1)}(\theta))
with
|E-E_{n,>}(\theta)|\leq\delta_{n-1}
. Fix
\operatorname{dist}(x,\{o,k_{m}\})\geq l_{m}^{6/7}
. Suppose
\|x\|_{1}\leq l_{m+2}/2
, restricting the eigenfunction equation to the
(m-1)
-good set
\Lambda:=B_{m+2}\setminus(B_{m-1}\cup(B_{m-1}+k_{m}))
together with the exponential decay of
G_{\Lambda}(E)
yields the desired decay of eigenfunctions. Suppose
l_{s}/2\leq\|x\|_{1}\leq l_{s+1}/2
for some
m+2\leq s\leq n-1
or
l_{n}/2\leq\|x\|_{1}
for
s=n
, restricting the eigenfunction equation to the
(s-1)
-good set
\Lambda:=B_{s+1}\setminus B_{s-1}
together with the exponential decay of
G_{\Lambda}(E)
yields the desired decay.
We explain that the above set
B_{s+1}\setminus B_{s-1}
is
(s-1)
-nonresonant for
m+2\leq s\leq n
since
I(E_{s-1})
is a single interval satisfying
|I(E_{s-1})|\lesssim\delta_{s-3}^{5}
, which together with Diophantine condition yields
\theta+x\cdot\omega\notin I(E_{s-1})
for all
x\in B_{s+1}\setminus B_{s-1}
. The
(s-1)
-regularity of
B_{s+1}\setminus B_{s-1}
is confirmed by the construction.

To prove (
3.59
), it suffices to show
\psi_{n+1,>}
and
\psi_{n+1,<}
are close to a linear combination of
\psi_{m,-}
and
\psi_{m,+}
inside
B_{m,-}\cup B_{m,+}
.
We restrict the equation
H_{B_{n+1}^{(1)}}(\theta)\psi_{n+1,>}=E_{n+1,>}(\theta)\psi_{n+1,>}
on
B_{m,-}
to get

\left(H_{B_{m,-}}-E_{n+1,>}\right)\psi_{n+1,>}=\Gamma_{B_{m,-}}\psi_{n+1,>}.

Thus

\|P_{m,-}^{\perp}\psi_{n+1,>}\|=\|G_{B_{m,-}}^{\perp}(E_{n+1,>})P_{m,-}^{\perp}\Gamma_{B_{m,-}}\psi_{n+1,>}\|=O(\delta_{m-1}^{-1}e^{-\frac{1}{4}\gamma_{0}l_{m}})\leq\delta_{m}^{10},

where
P_{m,-}^{\perp}=I-\langle\psi_{m,-}|\psi_{m,-}\rangle
is the projection onto the orthogonal complement of
\psi_{m,-}
and
G_{B_{m,-}}^{\perp}(E_{n+1,>})
is the Green’s function of
B_{m,-}
on
\operatorname{Image}P_{m,-}^{\perp}
with upper bound
O(\delta_{m-1}^{-1})
by
|E_{n+1,>}(\theta)-E_{m,-}(\theta)|\lesssim|J_{i_{m+1}}^{(1)}|\lesssim\delta_{m-1}^{5}
together with the eigenvalue separation result of simple resonance case (c.f. Proposition
3.27
replacing
n
by
m-1
).
Thus

\psi_{n+1,>}\chi_{B_{m,-}}=a\psi_{m,-}+O(\delta_{m}^{10}).

Similarly,

\psi_{n+1,>}\chi_{B_{m,+}}=b\psi_{m,+}+O(\delta_{m}^{10}).

The above estimates together with the exponential decay of the eigenfunction and orthogonality of
\psi_{n+1,>}
and
\psi_{n+1,<}
yield (
3.59
).

Since the eigenfunction corresponding to
E
in the interval
|E-E_{n,>}(\theta)|\leq\delta_{n-1}
has the form (
3.59
), there are only two such eigenvalues
E_{n+1,>}
and
E_{n+1,<}
or else it will violate the orthogonality.

Finally,
(c)
follows from
(a)
and
|E_{n+1,>}(\theta)-E_{n,>}(\theta)|,|E_{n+1,<}(\theta)-E_{n,>}(\theta)|\lesssim\delta_{n-1}^{5}
immediately.
∎

Define

P_{m,\pm}:=\langle\psi_{m,\pm}|\psi_{m,\pm}\rangle

and

Q_{s,\pm}:=\operatorname{Id}_{B_{s}}-P_{m,\pm},\ m+1\leq s\leq n+1.

and consider the compressed operators

H_{s,\pm}(\theta):=Q_{s,\pm}H_{B_{s}}(\theta)Q_{s,\pm}.

In the following proposition, we will use a multi-scale interlacing argument to construct the interlacing functions of
E_{s,>}(\theta)
and
E_{s,<}(\theta)
for all scales
m+1\leq s\leq n+1
. Unlike Lemma
3.35
which starts the construction at the scale
n
, the following proposition starts the construction at the scale
m
to obtain the interlacing functions
\lambda_{s,\mp}(\theta)
(
m+1\leq s\leq n+1
) scale by scale.

Proposition 3.45
.

For
m+1\leq s\leq n+1
and
\theta\in I^{(1)}_{\cup}
, the operators
H_{s,\pm}(\theta)
have unique eigenvalues
\lambda_{s,\mp}(\theta)
interlacing
E_{s,>}(\theta)
and
E_{s,<}(\theta)
:

E_{s,<}(\theta)\leq\lambda_{s,\mp}(\theta)\leq E_{s,>}(\theta),

(3.61)

and the eigenfunctions
\phi_{s,\mp}
corresponding to
\lambda_{s,\mp}
decay exponentially:

|\phi_{s,\mp}(x)|\leq e^{-\frac{\gamma_{0}}{5}\|x\|_{1}},\ \|x\|_{1}\geq l_{s}^{\frac{7}{8}}.

(3.62)

Moreover, we have

|\lambda_{s,\pm}^{\prime}(\theta)-E_{m,\pm}^{\prime}(\theta)|\lesssim e^{-\frac{1}{5}\gamma_{0}l_{m}}\delta_{m-1}^{-1}\ll\delta_{m}^{10}.

(3.63)

In particular,

\pm\lambda_{s,\pm}^{\prime}(\theta)\gtrsim\delta_{m-2}^{10}\gg\delta_{m-1}.

(3.64)

Proof.

We prove (
3.61
), (
3.62
) by induction. The case
s=m+1
was proved in Lemma
3.35
replacing
n
by
m
. Assuming that
\lambda_{s,\mp}
have been constructed, we are going to construct
\lambda_{s+1,\mp}
. First we claim that
H_{s+1,\pm}(\theta)
have unique eigenvalues
\lambda_{s+1,\mp}(\theta)
in
[\lambda_{s,\mp}(\theta)-\delta_{s-1},\lambda_{s,\mp}(\theta)+\delta_{s-1}]
satisfying

|\lambda_{s+1,\mp}(\theta)-\lambda_{s,\mp}(\theta)|\lesssim e^{-\frac{1}{5}\gamma_{0}l_{s}}.

(3.65)

Let
\phi_{s,\mp}\in\operatorname{Image}Q_{s,\pm}
be the eigenfunctions of
H_{s,\pm}(\theta)
corresponding to
\lambda_{s,\mp}(\theta)
.
By the exponential decay (
3.62
) for
\phi_{s,\mp}
, we have

\displaystyle\|(H_{s+1,\pm}(\theta)-\lambda_{s,\mp}(\theta))\phi_{s,\mp}\|

\displaystyle=\|Q_{s+1,\pm}(H_{B_{s+1}}(\theta)-\lambda_{s,\mp}(\theta))\phi_{s,\mp}\|

\displaystyle=\|Q_{s+1,\pm}\Gamma_{B_{s}}\phi_{s,\mp}\|\lesssim e^{-\frac{1}{5}\gamma_{0}l_{s}}.

The above estimate together with Lemma
2.2
yields the existence of
\lambda_{s+1,\mp}
.

Next we show the uniqueness and they are the desired interlacing curves. Let
\hat{\lambda}\in\sigma(H_{s+1,\pm})
satisfy
|\hat{\lambda}-\lambda_{s,\mp}|\leq\delta_{s-1}
with corresponding eigenfunction
\hat{\phi}\in\operatorname{Image}Q_{s+1,\pm}
. Then we have

Q_{s+1,\pm}(H_{B_{s+1}}-\hat{\lambda})\hat{\phi}=0.

Thus

(H_{B_{s+1}}-\hat{\lambda})\hat{\phi}=a\psi_{m,\pm}.

Employing the same argument as the proof of item
(b)
of Proposition
3.44
, together with the exponential decay of
\psi_{m,\pm}
, one can prove that

|\hat{\phi}(x)|\leq e^{-\frac{\gamma_{0}}{5}\|x\|_{1}}+e^{-\frac{\gamma_{0}}{5}\|x-k_{m}\|_{1}}

for
\operatorname{dist}(x,\{o,k_{m}\})\geq l_{m}^{7/8}
and
\hat{\phi}=\psi_{m,\mp}+O(e^{-\frac{1}{5}\gamma_{0}l_{m}}\delta_{m-1}^{-1})
since
\hat{\phi}\in\operatorname{Image}Q_{\pm}
.
Thus such an eigenvalue must be unique or else it will violate the orthogonality.
Since
E_{s,<}(\theta)\leq\lambda_{s,\mp}(\theta)\leq E_{s,>}(\theta)
and
E_{s+1,<},E_{s+1,>}
are generated from the interval
J_{i_{s}}^{(1)}
with
|J_{i_{s}}^{(1)}|\ll\delta_{s-1}
,
\lambda_{s+1,\mp}(\theta)
must lie between
E_{s+1,<}(\theta)
and
E_{s+1,>}(\theta)
by Theorem
3.14
. Thus we finish the proof of induction.

By the Feynman-Hellman formula
(1)
in Lemma
2.4
, we have

\displaystyle|\lambda_{s+1,\mp}^{\prime}-E_{m,\mp}^{\prime}|

\displaystyle=|\left\langle\phi_{s+1,\mp},(H_{s+1,\pm})^{\prime}\phi_{s+1,\mp}\right\rangle-\left\langle\psi_{m,\mp},H^{\prime}\psi_{m,\mp}\right\rangle|

\displaystyle=|\left\langle\phi_{s+1,\mp},V^{\prime}\phi_{s+1,\mp}\right\rangle-\left\langle\psi_{m,\mp},V^{\prime}\psi_{m,\mp}\right\rangle-2\langle\phi_{s+1,\mp},(P_{m,\pm})^{\prime}H_{B_{s+1}}\phi_{s+1,\mp}\rangle|

\displaystyle\leq|\left\langle\phi_{s+1,\mp},V^{\prime}\phi_{s+1,\mp}\right\rangle-\left\langle\psi_{m,\mp},V^{\prime}\psi_{m,\mp}\right\rangle|+2|\langle\phi_{s+1,\mp},(P_{m,\pm})^{\prime}H_{B_{s+1}}\phi_{s+1,\mp}\rangle|

\displaystyle\lesssim e^{-\frac{1}{5}\gamma_{0}l_{m}}\delta_{m-1}^{-1}+|\langle\phi_{s+1,\mp},Q_{s+1,\pm}(P_{m,\pm})^{\prime}H_{B_{s+1}}Q_{s+1,\pm}\phi_{s+1,\mp}\rangle|,

where we use the estimate
\|\phi_{s+1,\mp}-\psi_{m,\mp}\|\lesssim e^{-\frac{1}{5}\gamma_{0}l_{m}}\delta_{m-1}^{-1}
to bound the first term on the third line of the inequality.

Thus it suffices to estimate
Q_{s+1,\pm}P_{m,\pm}^{\prime}H_{B_{s+1}}Q_{s+1,\pm}
.
Recall that

\displaystyle P_{m,\pm}^{\prime}

\displaystyle=\langle\psi_{m,\pm}|\psi_{m,\pm}^{\prime}\rangle+\langle\psi_{m,\pm}^{\prime}|\psi_{m,\pm}\rangle

\displaystyle=-G_{B_{m,\pm}}^{\perp}(E_{m,\pm})V^{\prime}P_{m,\pm}-P_{m,\pm}V^{\prime}G_{B_{m,\pm}}^{\perp}(E_{m,\pm}).

Since
Q_{s+1,\pm}P_{m,\pm}=P_{m,\pm}Q_{s+1,\pm}=0
, it follows that

Q_{s+1,\pm}P_{m,\pm}^{\prime}H_{B_{s+1}}Q_{s+1,\pm}=-Q_{s+1,\pm}G_{B_{m,\pm}}^{\perp}(E_{m,\pm})V^{\prime}P_{m,\pm}H_{B_{s+1}}Q_{s+1,\pm}.

(3.66)

We can employ the estimates

\displaystyle\|P_{m,\pm}H_{B_{s+1}}Q_{s+1,\pm}\|

\displaystyle=\|P_{m,\pm}(H_{B_{s+1}}-E_{m,\pm})Q_{s+1,\pm}\|

\displaystyle\leq\|P_{m,\pm}(H_{B_{s+1}}-E_{m,\pm})\|

\displaystyle\leq\|\Gamma_{B_{m,\pm}}\psi_{m,\pm}\|\lesssim e^{-\frac{1}{5}\gamma_{0}l_{m}}

and
\|G_{B_{m,\pm}}^{\perp}(E_{m,\pm})\|=O(\delta_{m-1}^{-1})
to (
3.66
) and complete the proof of (
3.63
).

Finally, (
3.64
) follows from
\pm E_{m,\pm}^{\prime}\gtrsim\delta_{m-2}^{10}
and (
3.63
) immediately.
∎

Lemma 3.46
.

With the notation from Lemma
3.43
, there is a unique point
\bar{\theta}\in I^{(1)}_{\cup}
satisfying
|\bar{\theta}-\theta_{n,>}|\lesssim\delta_{n}\delta_{n-2}^{-2}
, such that

\lambda_{n+1,+}(\bar{\theta})=\lambda_{n+1,-}(\bar{\theta}).

Moreover, the Rellich children have quantitative separation away from
\bar{\theta}
:

E_{n+1,>}(\theta)-E_{n+1,<}(\theta)\geq|\lambda_{n+1,+}(\theta)-\lambda_{n+1,-}(\theta)|\geq\delta_{m-1}|\theta-\bar{\theta}|.

(3.67)

As a corollary, if
E_{n+1,>}(\theta)=E_{n+1,<}(\theta)
, then
\theta=\bar{\theta}
.

Proof.

We consider the difference function

d(\theta):=\lambda_{n+1,+}(\theta)-\lambda_{n+1,-}(\theta).

By (
3.64
), we have

d^{\prime}(\theta)\geq 2\delta_{m-1}.

(3.68)

Recalling (
3.55
), it follows that

\displaystyle|\lambda_{n,+}(\theta_{n,>})-\lambda_{n,-}(\theta_{n,>})|

\displaystyle\leq E_{n,>}(\theta_{n,>})-E_{n,<}(\theta_{n,>})

\displaystyle\leq E_{n,>}(\theta_{n,>})-E_{n,<}(\theta_{n,<})+|E_{n,<}(\theta_{n,<})-E_{n,<}(\theta_{n,>})|

\displaystyle\leq 3\delta_{n}+O(\delta_{n}\delta_{n-2}^{-1}).

By the above estimate and approximation (
3.65
), we obtain

|d(\theta_{n,>})|=|\lambda_{n+1,+}(\theta_{n,>})-\lambda_{n+1,-}(\theta_{n,>})|\lesssim\delta_{n}\delta_{n-2}^{-1}.

It follows that by mean value theorem there exists a unique point
\bar{\theta}
satisfying
|\bar{\theta}-\theta_{n,>}|\lesssim\delta_{n}\delta_{n-2}^{-1}\delta_{m-1}^{-1}\leq\delta_{n}\delta_{n-2}^{-2}
, such that

d(\bar{\theta})=0
, that is

\lambda_{n+1,+}(\bar{\theta})=\lambda_{n+1,-}(\bar{\theta}).

Then (
3.67
) follows from (
3.61
), (
3.68
) and

|\lambda_{n+1,+}(\theta)-\lambda_{n+1,-}(\theta)|=|d(\theta)|=|d(\theta)-d(\bar{\theta})|\geq\delta_{m-1}|\theta-\bar{\theta}|

by mean value theorem.
∎

Suppose that
|E_{m,+}^{\prime}(\theta_{k_{m},-})|\geq|E_{m,-}^{\prime}(\theta_{k_{m},-})|
(the argument is similar to the opposite case), and define
1\leq r\lesssim\delta_{m-1}^{-1}
such that

|E_{m,+}^{\prime}(\theta_{k_{m},-})|=r|E_{n,-}^{\prime}(\theta_{k_{m},-})|.

Recalling Lemma
3.37
, for
\theta\in I_{\cup}^{(1)}
, we have

|(E_{m,+}^{\prime}+rE_{m,-}^{\prime})(\theta)|\leq\delta_{m-1}^{2}.

(3.69)

Proposition 3.47
.

For
\theta\in I_{\cup}^{(1)}
, we have the following:

(a).

If
E_{n+1,>}(\theta)\neq E_{n+1,<}(\theta)
, then

\displaystyle(E_{n+1,>})^{\prime}

\displaystyle=(A^{2}-rB^{2})E_{m,-}^{\prime}+O(\delta_{m-1}^{2}),

(3.70)

\displaystyle(E_{n+1,<})^{\prime}

\displaystyle=(B^{2}-rA^{2})E_{m,-}^{\prime}+O(\delta_{m-1}^{2}),

(3.71)

and

\displaystyle(E_{n+1,>})^{\prime\prime}

\displaystyle=\frac{2\left\langle\psi_{n+1},V^{\prime}\Psi_{n+1}\right\rangle^{2}}{E_{n+1,>}-E_{n+1,<}}+O(\delta_{n-1}^{-1}),

(3.72)

\displaystyle(E_{n+1,<})^{\prime\prime}

\displaystyle=\frac{2\left\langle\psi_{n+1},V^{\prime}\Psi_{n+1}\right\rangle^{2}}{E_{n+1,<}-E_{n+1,>}}+O(\delta_{n-1}^{-1})

(3.73)

with the notation from Proposition
3.44
and
r
as above.

(b).

Suppose
E_{n+1,>}(\theta)\neq E_{n+1,<}(\theta)
. If
|(E_{n+1,>})^{\prime}(\theta)|\leq\delta_{m-1}^{2}
, then
(E_{n+1,>})^{\prime\prime}(\theta)\geq 2
. Similarly, if
|(E_{n+1,<})^{\prime}(\theta)|\leq\delta_{m-1}^{2}
, then
(E_{n+1,<})^{\prime\prime}(\theta)\leq-2
.

(c).

If there is a level crossing (thus
E_{n+1,>}(\bar{\theta})=E_{n+1,<}(\bar{\theta})
by Lemma
3.46
), then
E_{n+1,>},E_{n+1,<}
are piecewise differentiable (except the point
\bar{\theta}
), and for
\theta>\bar{\theta}
,

(E_{n+1,>})^{\prime}(\theta)>\delta_{m-1}^{2},\ (E_{n+1,<})^{\prime}(\theta)<-\delta_{m-1}^{2},

for
\theta<\bar{\theta}
,

(E_{n+1,>})^{\prime}(\theta)<-\delta_{m-1}^{2},\ (E_{n+1,<})^{\prime}(\theta)>\delta_{m-1}^{2}.

Proof.

We are going to prove (
3.70
). (The proof of (
3.71
) is analogous.) By (
3.59
) and (
3.69
), we refer to Feynman-Hellman formula
(1)
Lemma
2.4
to obtain

\displaystyle(E_{n+1,>})^{\prime}

\displaystyle=\left\langle\psi_{n+1},V^{\prime}\psi_{n+1}\right\rangle=A^{2}\left\langle\psi_{m,-},V^{\prime}\psi_{m,-}\right\rangle+B^{2}\left\langle\psi_{m,+},V^{\prime}\psi_{m,+}\right\rangle+O(\delta_{m}^{10})

\displaystyle=A^{2}E_{m,-}^{\prime}+B^{2}E_{m,+}^{\prime}+O(\delta_{m}^{10})

\displaystyle=(A^{2}-rB^{2})E_{m,-}^{\prime}+B^{2}(E_{m,+}^{\prime}+rE_{m,-}^{\prime})+O(\delta_{m}^{10})

\displaystyle=(A^{2}-rB^{2})E_{m,-}^{\prime}+O(\delta_{m-1}^{2}).

To prove (
3.72
) and (
3.73
), we use Feynman-Hellman formula
(2)
and
(3)
to obtain

(E_{n+1,>})^{\prime\prime}=\left\langle\psi_{n+1},V^{\prime\prime}\psi_{n+1}\right\rangle+2\frac{\left\langle\psi_{n+1},V^{\prime}\Psi_{n+1}\right\rangle^{2}}{E_{n+1,>}-E_{n+1,<}}-2\left\langle V^{\prime}\psi_{n+1},G^{\perp\perp}_{n+1}(E_{n+1,>})V^{\prime}\psi_{n+1}\right\rangle.

The first term is bounded by
D
and the third term is bounded by

2\|G^{\perp\perp}_{n+1}(E_{n+1,>})\|\|V^{\prime}\psi_{n+1}\|^{2}\lesssim\delta_{n-1}^{-1},

where we use the estimate
\|G^{\perp\perp}_{n+1}(E_{n+1,>})\|\leq 2\delta_{n-1}^{-1}
from item
(c)
of Proposition
3.44
. Thus we finish the proof of item
(a)
.

Now we are going to prove
(b)
. We will show the first term in (
3.72
) is large if
|(E_{n+1,>})^{\prime}(\theta)|\leq\delta_{m-1}^{2}
.

Assume
|(E_{n+1,>})^{\prime}(\theta)|\leq\delta_{m-1}^{2}
, by (
3.70
), we have

|A^{2}-rB^{2}||E_{m,-}^{\prime}(\theta)|\leq|(E_{n+1,>})^{\prime}(\theta)|+O(\delta_{m-1}^{2})\lesssim\delta_{m-1}^{2}.

Since

|E_{m,-}^{\prime}(\theta)|\gtrsim\delta_{m-2}^{10},

it follows that

|A^{2}-rB^{2}|\lesssim\delta_{m-1}^{2}\delta_{m-2}^{-10}<\frac{1}{100}

Since
A^{2}+B^{2}=1
and
r\geq 1
, we obtain

(1+r)B^{2}\geq A^{2}+B^{2}-|A^{2}-rB^{2}|\geq\frac{99}{100}

and

(1+\frac{1}{r})A^{2}\geq A^{2}+B^{2}-\frac{1}{r}|A^{2}-rB^{2}|\geq\frac{99}{100}.

Thus

B^{2}\geq\frac{1}{4r},\ A^{2}\geq\frac{1}{4}.

By (
3.59
), (
3.69
) and the previous lower bound for
A,B
, we have

\displaystyle|\langle\psi_{n+1},V^{\prime}\Psi_{n+1}\rangle|

\displaystyle=|AB(\langle\psi_{m,-},V^{\prime}\psi_{m,-}\rangle-\langle\psi_{m,+},V^{\prime}\psi_{m,+}\rangle)+O(\delta_{m}^{10})|

\displaystyle=|AB(E_{m,-}^{\prime}-E_{m,+}^{\prime})+O(\delta_{m}^{10})|

\displaystyle=|AB\left((1+r)E_{m,-}^{\prime}-(E_{m,+}^{\prime}+rE_{m,-}^{\prime})\right)+O(\delta_{m}^{10})|

\displaystyle\geq\frac{1+r}{4\sqrt{r}}(|E_{m,-}^{\prime}|-O(\delta_{m-1}^{2}))

\displaystyle\gtrsim\delta_{m-2}^{10}\gg\delta_{m-1}.

The denominator
0<E_{n+1,>}-E_{n+1,<}\lesssim\delta_{n-1}^{5}
since
|J_{i_{n}}^{(1)}|\lesssim\delta_{n-1}^{10}
and
|I_{\cup}^{(1)}|\lesssim\delta_{n-1}^{5}
. Combining the previous estimate of numerator, by (
3.72
), we obtain

(E_{n+1,>})^{\prime\prime}(\theta)\gtrsim\delta_{m-1}^{2}\delta_{n-1}^{-5}-O(\delta_{n-1}^{-1})>2,

and

-(E_{n+1,<})^{\prime\prime}(\theta)\gtrsim\delta_{m-1}^{2}\delta_{n-1}^{-5}-O(\delta_{n-1}^{-1})>2.

Supposing there is a level crossing, then

E_{n+1,>}(\bar{\theta})=\lambda_{n+1,+}(\bar{\theta})=\lambda_{n+1,-}(\bar{\theta})=E_{n+1,<}(\bar{\theta}).

Thus for
\theta>\bar{\theta}
,

E_{n+1,>}(\theta)\geq\lambda_{n+1,+}(\theta)>\lambda_{n+1,-}(\theta)\geq E_{n+1,<}(\theta).

The above inequality together with (
3.64
) yields the left derivative estimates of
E_{n+1,>}
and
E_{n+1,<}
, namely,

(E_{n+1,>})^{\prime}_{\operatorname{Left}}(\bar{\theta})\geq\lambda_{n+1,+}^{\prime}(\bar{\theta})>\delta_{m-1},\ (E_{n+1,<})^{\prime}_{\operatorname{Left}}(\bar{\theta})\leq\lambda_{n+1,-}^{\prime}(\bar{\theta})<-\delta_{m-1}.

We next claim the inequalities hold for all
\theta>\bar{\theta}
. If it is not true, then

\{\theta\in I_{\cup}^{(1)}:\ \theta>\bar{\theta},\ \ (E_{n+1,>})^{\prime}(\theta)\leq\delta_{m-1}^{2}\}\neq\emptyset.

Let
\theta^{*}
be its infimum. Since
E_{n+1,>}^{\prime}(\theta)
is continuous for
\theta\geq\bar{\theta}
and
(E_{n+1,>})^{\prime}_{\operatorname{Left}}(\bar{\theta})>\delta_{m-1}^{2}
, we have
\theta^{*}>\bar{\theta}
and
(E_{n+1,>})^{\prime}(\theta)>\delta_{m-1}^{2}\geq(E_{n+1,>})^{\prime}(\theta^{*})
for
\theta\in[\bar{\theta},\theta^{*})
, which implies
(E_{n+1,>})^{\prime\prime}(\theta^{*})\leq 0
. However, by item
(b)
, we get a contradiction that
(E_{n+1,>})^{\prime\prime}(\theta^{*})>2
. Thus we prove our claim. The case of
\theta<\bar{\theta}
is analogous.
∎

Remark 3.9
.

We can deduce from the above proposition that
E_{n+1,>}
and
E_{n+1,<}
have the two-monotonicity interval structure. Moreover, if the gap between the critical values of
E_{n+1,>}
and
E_{n+1,<}
is larger than
3\delta_{n+1}
, then each of them belongs to
Type

2
, otherwise,
E_{n+1,>}
along with
E_{n+1,<}
belong to
Type

3
. We finish the proof of
Subcase B
.

3.5.4.
A sketch of the domain adjustment

At the end of the construction, we employ the argument in Section
3.2.3
to adjust the domains of Rellich children by
O(\delta_{n}^{10})
so that every Rellich child
E_{n+1}
has the same image on each of its monotone interval. After the domain adjustment, Hypothesis
5
still holds for
m=n+1
because the overlap of the adjacent codomains (
\sim 3\delta_{n}
) is much larger than the codomain contraction in the adjustment. We henceforth denote by
I(E_{n+1})
(resp.
J(E_{n+1})
) the modified domain (codomain) of
E_{n+1}
.

3.5.5.
Green’s function estimates

In this part, we establish the Green’s function estimates for
(n+1)
-good sets by a multi-scale analysis argument and finish the proof of induction.

Recall that
\tilde{J}(E_{n+1})
is defined as:
For
E_{n+1}
belonging to
Type

1
or
2
,

(1).
if
E_{n+1}
has no critical point in
I(E_{n+1})
, then

\tilde{J}(E_{n+1}):=[\inf J(E_{n+1})+\frac{9}{8}\delta_{n},\sup J(E_{n+1})-\frac{9}{8}\delta_{n}],

(2).
if
E_{n+1}
has its minimum at a critical point in
I(E_{n+1})
, then

\tilde{J}(E_{n+1}):=[\inf J(E_{n+1})-2\delta_{n+1},\sup J(E_{n+1})-\frac{9}{8}\delta_{n}],

(3).
if
E_{n+1}
has its maximum at a critical point in
I(E_{n+1})
, then

\tilde{J}(E_{n+1}):=[\inf J(E_{n+1})+\frac{9}{8}\delta_{n},\sup J(E_{n+1})+2\delta_{n+1}],

and for
E_{n+1}
belonging to
Type

3
,

\tilde{J}(E_{n+1}):=[\inf J(E_{n+1,<})+\frac{9}{8}\delta_{n},\sup J(E_{n+1,>})-\frac{9}{8}\delta_{n}].

Fix
\theta^{*},E^{*}
. Let
E_{n+1}\in\mathcal{C}_{n+1}
be the Rellich function such that
E^{*}\in\tilde{J}(E_{n+1})
(if exists). The set of
(n+1)
-resonant points (relative to
\theta^{*},E^{*}
and
E_{n+1}
) is defined as: For
E_{n+1}
belonging to
Type

1
or
2
,

S_{n+1}(\theta^{*},E^{*}):=\{x\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}:\ \theta^{*}+x\cdot\omega\in I(E_{n+1}),|E_{n+1}(\theta^{*}+x\cdot\omega)-E^{*}|<\delta_{n+1}\},

and for
E_{n+1}
belonging to
Type

3
,

\displaystyle\ \ \ S_{n+1}(\theta^{*},E^{*})

\displaystyle:=\{x\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}:\ \theta^{*}+x\cdot\omega\in I(E_{n+1,<})\cup I(E_{n+1,>}),\min_{\bullet\in\{>,<\}}|E_{n+1,\bullet}(\theta^{*}+x\cdot\omega)-E^{*}|<\delta_{n+1}\}.

We say that a set
\Lambda\subset\mathbb{Z}^{d}
is
(n+1)
-nonresonant (relative to
(\theta^{*},E^{*})
and
E_{n+1}
) if
\Lambda\cap S_{n+1}(\theta^{*},E^{*})=\emptyset
and is
(n+1)
-regular if
(\Lambda_{2l_{i+1}}+x)\subset\Lambda
for any
x\in S_{i}(\theta^{*},E^{*})\cap\Lambda
relative to each ancestor
E_{i}

(0\leq i\leq n)
of
E_{n+1}
. We say that a set is
(n+1)
-good if it is both
(n+1)
-nonresonant and
(n+1)
-regular. We are going to prove Green’s function estimates for
(n+1)
-good sets:

Theorem 3.48
.

Fix
\theta^{*},E^{*}
and a finite set
\Lambda\subset\mathbb{Z}^{d}
. Let
E_{n+1}\in\mathcal{C}_{n+1}
satisfy
E^{*}\in\tilde{J}(E_{n+1})
(if exists). If
\Lambda
is
(n+1)
-good, then for
|\theta-\theta^{*}|<\delta_{n+1}/(10D),|E-E^{*}|<\delta_{n+1}/5
,

\displaystyle\|G_{\Lambda}(\theta,E)\|

\displaystyle\leq 10\delta_{n+1}^{-1},

\displaystyle|G_{\Lambda}(\theta,E;x,y)|

\displaystyle\leq e^{-\gamma_{n+1}\|x-y\|_{1}},\ \|x-y\|_{1}\geq l_{n+1}^{\frac{5}{6}},

where
\gamma_{n+1}=(1-O(l_{n+1}^{-\frac{1}{30}}))\gamma_{n}\geq\frac{1}{2}\gamma_{0}
.
The above estimates also hold if
\Lambda
is
(n+1)
-regular and
E^{*}\notin\tilde{J}(E_{n+1})
for any
E_{n+1}\in\mathcal{C}_{n+1}
.

Proof.

If
E^{*}\notin\tilde{J}(E_{n})
for any
E_{n}\in\mathcal{C}_{n}
, then the theorem follows from the induction hypothesis. By the overlap of two adjacent modified codomains
\tilde{J}
where the Rellich functions do not attain their critical values (c.f. Hypothesis
5
), it follows that
\cup_{E_{n}\in\mathcal{C}_{n}}\tilde{J}(E_{n})=\cup_{E_{n}\in\mathcal{C}_{n}}\tilde{\tilde{J}}(E_{n})
. We may thus assume
E^{*}\in\tilde{\tilde{J}}(E_{n})
for some
E_{n}\in\mathcal{C}_{n}
.

First, we assume that
E^{*}\in\tilde{J}(E_{n+1})\subset\tilde{J}(E_{n})
for some
E_{n+1}\in\mathcal{C}^{(j)}(E_{n})
. We define the set
S_{n}^{\Lambda}:=S_{n}(\theta^{*},E^{*})\cap\Lambda
. If
S_{n}^{\Lambda}=\emptyset
, then
\Lambda
is
n
-nonresonant and the theorem follows from the induction hypothesis. We thus assume
S_{n}^{\Lambda}
is nonempty. We wish to find a finite family
\{B_{n+1}^{(j)}+p\}_{p\in\bar{S}_{n}^{\Lambda}}
of translations of
B_{n+1}^{(j)}
such that each
x\in S_{n}^{\Lambda}
is near the center of
B_{n+1}^{(j)}+p
for some
p\in\bar{S}_{n}^{\Lambda}
and
\|G_{B_{n+1}^{(j)}+p}(\theta^{*},E^{*})\|\leq\delta_{n+1}^{-1}

for all
p\in\bar{S}_{n}^{\Lambda}
. We do so by case analysis:

(1).

j=1
: We claim that for any
x\in S_{n}^{\Lambda}
, if
E_{n+1}
is generated from the interval in
Subcase A
, then
\theta^{*}+x\cdot\omega\in I(E_{n+1})
, and if
E_{n+1}
is generated from the interval (
3.54
) from
Subcase B
, then
\theta^{*}+x\cdot\omega\in I(E_{n+1,<})\cup I(E_{n+1,>})
, where
E_{n+1,<}
and
E_{n+1,>}
are the two Rellich children generated from the interval (
3.54
). The proof proceeds also by case analysis:

Subcase A (a).

E_{n+1}
has no critical point.

Since
E^{*}\in\tilde{J}(E_{n+1})
and
x\in S_{n}^{\Lambda}
, by the definition of
\tilde{J}(E_{n+1})
, we obtain

E_{n}(\theta^{*}+x\cdot\omega)\in[\inf J(E_{n+1})+\frac{1}{8}\delta_{n},\sup J(E_{n+1})-\frac{1}{8}\delta_{n}].

Since
j=1
, by approximation (
3.34
), we have

[\inf J(E_{n+1})+\frac{1}{8}\delta_{n},\sup J(E_{n+1})-\frac{1}{8}\delta_{n}]\subset(E_{n})_{\pm}(I(E_{n+1})_{\pm}).

Thus
E_{n}(\theta^{*}+x\cdot\omega)\in(E_{n})_{\pm}(I(E_{n+1})_{\pm})
and so
\theta^{*}+x\cdot\omega\in I(E_{n+1})
.

Subcase A (b).

E_{n+1}
has a critical point.

Suppose that this critical point is a minimum (the other case is analogous). Then
E_{n}
likewise achieves its minimum at a critical point in
I(E_{n+1})
. As above, we have

E_{n}(\theta^{*}+x\cdot\omega)\in[\inf J(E_{n}),\sup J(E_{n+1})-\frac{1}{8}\delta_{n}]

and
